as Gritsenko~$1999$ Theorem~$1.2$ paramodular denominator weights on CHL $K3 \times E$ orbifolds with $\varphi(N) \mid 2$.
\item[Gritsenko--Cl\'ery atlas scope.] The eight-form catalogue is not
indexed by the CHL order $N$.  Its rows are the commuting-pair /
level-indexed triples
\[
 (t,N_{\mathrm{lvl}};k)\in
\{(1,1;5),(2,1;2),(1,2;3),(3,1;1),
(1,3;2),(4,1;1/2),(1,4;3/2),(2,2;1)\}.
\]
The weight is again the Borcherds half-constant in that row's
normalisation, but the row label is $(t,N_{\mathrm{lvl}})$, not the
CHL orbifold order.
\end{description}
The two scopes agree only when the input Jacobi form and the lattice
normalisation agree.  Non-CHL hosts at the CY$_3$ level are
conjectural and must carry their own comparison package; they are not
obtained by extending the CHL order set by fixed-locus arithmetic.

\smallskip

\emph{Two paramodular families on the CHL index set.} The
BKM-denominator-scope tuple $(5, 4, 3, 2, 1)$ is the CHL-averaged
paramodular weight $\mathrm{wt}(\Delta_1^{(N)})$ of Gritsenko~$1999$
Thm.~$1.2$ (additive lift of the weight-$k(N)$ index-$1$ Jacobi input).
Other twined Borcherds lifts use different Jacobi inputs and therefore
different constants.  The default on the symbol $\kBKM$ in this
programme is the CHL-averaged paramodular convention unless a
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery row is explicitly named by its $(t,N_{\mathrm{lvl}})$
label.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Additivity \texorpdfstring{$\kBKM = \kch + \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{fibre}})$}{kBKM = kch + chi(O-fibre)} fails at every \texorpdfstring{$N$}{N}]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-platonic-additivity-fails}
\index{Additivity!kappa_BKM vs kappa_ch plus chi(O_fibre) fails}
The tempting additive formula $\kBKM(\Phi_N) = \kch + \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{fibre}})$ fails at every $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$: at $N = 1$ the right-hand side is $\kch(K3 \times E) + \chi(\cO_E) = 0 + 0 = 0$, while the left is $5$; at $N = 2$ the right is $1$, while the left is $4$. The universal law is $\kBKM(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-cy3-platonic-universal-kBKM}); the apparent coincidence $5 = 3 + 2$ at $N = 1$ reads $5 = \kch^{\mathrm{Heis}} + \kcat(K3)$, mixing the rank-additive Heisenberg characteristic with the categorical Euler characteristic of the fibre and cannot extend: the identification $\kch^{\mathrm{Heis}}$ is additive under fibrations, while $\kcat$ is K\"unneth-multiplicative on total spaces and gives $\kcat(K3 \times E) = 0$. The five canonical constructions $\{\kcat, \kch^{\mathrm{Hodge}}, \kch^{\mathrm{Heis}}, \kBKM, \kfib\}$ on $K3 \times E$ each answer a different question on the same geometry (with $\kcat$ and $\kch^{\mathrm{Hodge}}$ coinciding numerically at $0$ on the compact CY$_3$ yet distinct as constructions, the K\"unneth-multiplicative categorical Euler vs.\ the chiral Hodge supertrace); they do not combine additively.
\end{remark}

\subsection{AdS\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} throat and dyon counting}
\label{subsec:k3e-cy3-platonic-ads3}

\begin{theorem}[AdS\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}/CFT\texorpdfstring{$_2$}{-2} throat and dyon degeneracy on the CHL tower]
\label{thm:k3e-cy3-platonic-ads3}
\index{AdS3!dyon counting throat}
For each $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ the Type~IIB near-horizon throat of the $D1$-$D5$-$p$ CHL black hole on $K3 \times S^1$ is
\[
 \mathrm{AdS}_3 \times S^3/\ZZ_N \times K3^{g_N}, \qquad c_N \;=\; 24 k_N, \qquad k_N \;=\; \frac{24}{N + 1} - 2,
\]
so $(k_1, \ldots, k_6) = (10, -, 6, 4, -, 2)$ and the CHL slice $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ gives $(k_N)_N = (10, 6, 4, 3, 2)$. The $1/4$-BPS dyon partition function on the paramodular contour $\mathcal{C}_N$ in the Sen attractor chamber is
\[
 d_N(Q, P) \;=\; \oint_{\mathcal{C}_N} \frac{\exp\!\bigl(-i\pi\,(Q \cdot \Omega)\bigr)}{\Phi_{k_N}(\Omega)^2}\,d\rho\,d\sigma\,dv,
\]
with $\Phi_{k_N}$ the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery paramodular form of weight $k_N$. The leading-$D$ entropy is
\[
 \log d_N \;=\; 2\pi\sqrt{\Delta/N} \;+\; (k_N + 2)\,\log\Delta \;+\; \text{subleading},
\]
with $\Delta = Q^2 P^2 - (Q\cdot P)^2$; at $N = 1$ this recovers the Sen~$2008$ $2\pi\sqrt{\Delta}$ area law with twelve-dimensional logarithmic correction.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Sen~$2008$ (Rademacher expansion at $N = 1$); DVV~$1997$ (dyon partition function at $N = 1$); Shih--Strominger--Yin~$2005$ (CHL extension at $N \in \{2, 3, 4, 6\}$); Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier~$2012$ Theorem~$1.1$ (arithmetic Rademacher expansion); $k_N = 24/(N+1) - 2$ is Jatkar--Sen~$2005$; $c_N = 24 k_N$ is Strominger--Vafa~$1996$ combined with Brown--Henneaux~$1986$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Pure mathematical bridge datum for the AdS\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{3} statement]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-ads3-pure-math-bridge}
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-cy3-platonic-ads3} is proved inside the
standard CHL/D1--D5--$p$ physical bridge: the arithmetic input
($\Phi_{k_N}$, the paramodular contour, and the Rademacher expansion)
is explicit. As a theorem of the CY-to-chiral programme it remains
conditional on the pure mathematical holographic bridge datum of
Definition~\ref{def:pure-math-holographic-bridge-datum}. The current
K3$\times E$/CHL data supply the charge lattice, BPS index character,
Rademacher asymptotic, and physical duality frame; they do not yet
construct the pure functor
$\mathsf{BPS}_{K3\times E}\to\mathsf{Bdry}_{K3\times E}$, the product
comparison carrying Hall convolution to chiral OPE, or the
orientation-line coherence identifying the physical BPS trace with the
boundary chiral trace. The black-hole and AdS statements are therefore
arithmetic/physical theorems and conditional CY-to-chiral bridge
consequences.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Graviton finiteness \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{HH}^2_{E_2}$}{HH-2-E-2}-vanishing]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-platonic-graviton-HH2}
\index{Graviton finiteness!equals HH^2 E_2 vanishing}
The physical statement ``the near-horizon single-graviton algebra is finite-dimensional at fixed mass level'' is, at the chiral-boundary level, the algebraic statement $\mathrm{HH}^2_{E_2}(A_{K3}, A_{K3}) = 0$ in the $E_2$-Hochschild bicomplex of the K3 chiral algebra: no first-order $E_2$-deformations preserve CY structure. The reading is simultaneously chain-level (explicit Hochschild complex of the lattice VOA at rank~$24$) and $(\infty, 1)$-categorical (the stable $\infty$-category of $A_{K3}$-modules has its $\mathrm{End}^{\chir}$-shifted obstruction class in degree~$2$ vanishing). Both lanes are load-bearing: the chain-level $\mathrm{HH}^2 = 0$ computation is what makes the physical finiteness quantitative; the $(\infty, 1)$-categorical reading is what places the identification inside the CY-to-chiral functor framework.
\end{remark}

\section*{Closing crystallisation}
\label{sec:k3e-cy3-closing-crystallisation}

Theorem~\ref{thm:shadow-siegel-gap} resolves the gap between
the shadow tower of the $\cN = 4$ sigma model on $K3$ and the
Siegel-modular partition function \(1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) of \(K3 \times E\)
into four obstruction classes
$(\mathrm{O}_1, \mathrm{O}_2, \mathrm{O}_3, \mathrm{O}_4)$.
The five $\kappa_\bullet$-values
$\{\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}},
\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}},
\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}\} = \{0, 0, 3, 5, 24\}$
\textup{(}Remark~\ref{rem:four-kappas}\textup{)} are the scalar
remnants of this factorisation: each answers a different
categorical, Hodge-supertrace, Koszul, Borcherds, or fibrewise
question, and each arises from a different construction on the
same geometry. The chiral Hodge supertrace
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(K3 \times E) =
\sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}(K3 \times E) = 0$ is the canonical fifth
construction; it coincides numerically with $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = 0$
on the compact CY$_3$ yet is a distinct construction (chiral Hodge
supertrace vs.\ K\"unneth-multiplicative categorical Euler), and
both entries are load-bearing in the full $\{0, 0, 3, 5, 24\}$
spectrum. Of the seven faces, the Borcherds algebra
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ is the only one that survives the
genus-$2$ bridge intact; the shadow correction factor
$R_{\mathrm{shadow}}^{K3}$ of
Theorem~\ref{conj:genus2-k3e-full} encodes the first-quantised
$A_\infty$ content invisible to DMVV. The six routes to the quantum
vertex chiral group $G(K3 \times E)$ remain six distinct constructions
\textup{(}not six applications of $\Phi_3$\textup{)}; their
common image, after the bridge and centre/double data are supplied, is
the content of CY-C.



\section{\texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E} synthesis}
\label{sec:k3e-cy3-synthesis}

\begin{remark}[Bi-based factorisation datum, synthesis presentation]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-bi-based-synthesis}
The K3 chiral bialgebra of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic} lives on the bi-based Ran-space datum of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-bi-based-factorization}: chiral base $\mathrm{Ran}(E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24})$ on the smooth locus of the $24$-node discriminant curve with nearby-cycles $\mathcal{D}$-modules at the $24$ Kodaira $I_1$-nodes; parameter base $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ with Francis--Gaitsgory factorisation $\infty$-category in holonomic $\mathcal{D}$-modules regular-singular on $H_1\cup H_4$; averaging morphism $\mathrm{av}=\mathrm{Torelli}_{K3}\circ\mathrm{KugaSatake}\circ j_{\mathrm{Kodaira}}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta-5} as \texorpdfstring{$1$}{1}-loop-forced output]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-one-loop}
The Gritsenko--Nikulin form $\Delta_5$ is the $1$-loop-forced output of paramodular anomaly cancellation in twisted 11D supergravity on $K3\times T^2$ with $24$ M5-branes on $I_1$ Kodaira fibres. Its weight is the Borcherds constant-term value $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=c_1(0)/2=10/2=5$, not an additive decomposition of $\chi(\mathcal{O}_{K3})$ plus a residue sum. Four duality frames (heterotic Harvey--Moore $1996$; IIB D1-D5 DVV $1996$-$97$; IIA DMVV $1997$; M-theory Witten $1995$) produce the same form. Stated as Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-1loop-output} in Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$4d$}{4d} class-\texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{S}$}{S} parent]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-classs}
The $4d$ $\mathcal{N}=2$ parent is $\mathcal{T}[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]$ with $24$ maximal regular $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ punctures preserving the Steiner system $S(5,8,24)$ (Conway--Sloane $1988$). The Chacaltana--Distler pants decomposition ($22$ trinions, $21$ tubes; trinion $T_2$ with $n_v = 0$, $n_h = 4$; $(n_v(\mathcal{T}), n_h(\mathcal{T})) = (63, 88)$ at $(g, n) = (0, 24)$) gives $c_{4d}(A_1, \Sigma_{0,n}, \text{max reg}) = (5n - 13)/6 = (2n_v + n_h)/12$, so at $(g, n) = (0, 24)$: $(c_{4d}, c_{2d}) = (107/6, -214)$ via Beem--Rastelli $c_{2d} = -12 c_{4d}$. Coulomb rank $21$; flavour $\mathfrak{su}(2)^{24}$. The Schur-to-Borcherds bridge is the composite of Theorem~\ref{thm:schur-to-delta5-composite}: $M_{24}$-average the Schur index to $\phi_{0,1}^{K3}$, then apply the Borcherds lift to $\Delta_5$; it is not a direct equality with $1/\Delta_5$. Primary: Chacaltana--Distler $2010$ \S 5.14; Shapere--Tachikawa $2008$ \S 3; Beem--Rastelli $2015$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3xE} holographic six-layer register]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-six-layer-holographic-register}
The six-layer tower of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-six-layer-holographic-stratification} resolves the
holographic residual into five proved computations and one
named pure-bridge datum. The class-\(\mathcal S\) layer computes
\[
 (n_v,n_h)=(63,88),\qquad
 c_{4d}=107/6,\qquad
 c_{2d}=-214,
\]
and the Drinfeld--Sokolov/BRST gluing is the \(2\)d avatar of the same
pants-decomposition anomaly balance. The hCS layer is proved
locally by Costello--Paquette and Costello--Yagi, but its compact
\(K3\times E\) use as a \(\Phi_3\)-boundary theorem requires the pure
holographic bridge datum
\(\mathfrak H_{K3\times E}^{\mathrm{mat}}\). The Miki layer is the
local \(S_3\)-triality of toroidal charts
\((q_1,q_2,q_3)\), \(q_1q_2q_3=1\); its global compact reading is the
compatibility of that triality with the Hall--Borcherds centre. The
AdS/dyon layer is the automorphic theorem
\[
 \Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2,\qquad
 \mathrm{wt}(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})=10,\qquad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})=5.
\]
The Gritsenko--Cl\'ery throat layer is the lattice-polarised atlas
indexed by triples $(t, N; k) \in \{(1,1;5),(2,1;2),(1,2;3),(3,1;1),(1,3;2),
(4,1;\tfrac12),(1,4;\tfrac32),(2,2;1)\}$ with weight tuple
\[
 (5, 2, 3, 1, 2, \tfrac12, \tfrac32, 1)
\]
(Theorem~\ref{thm:eight-form-cy-host-catalogue},
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery~$2008$ arXiv:0812.3962), with non-CHL cells kept
as automorphic throat data until CY hosts and boundary functors are
constructed. The register forbids the three
false identifications: \(c_{2d}=-214\) is not
\(\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}\); \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) has weight \(10\), not \(5\);
Miki triality acts on local toroidal charts, not on the global
\(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\) denominator without the Hall--Borcherds
centre compatibility.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[Conditional Heegner classification invariant]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-classification}
Assume the same compact-source recognition and Bruinier--Heegner
characteristic comparison as in
Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-cy3-platonic-classification}.  The \(K(1)\)-invariant
classification line then has scalar image \(\mathbb C\cdot\Delta_5\).
Without that comparison package the Igusa cusp form is a target
automorphic invariant, not an already computed cohomology class of
\(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Mukai doubling and the order-eight conductor]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-mukai}
The $\mathcal B$-family conductor is
\[
K_{\mathcal B}=2c_+(\mathrm{Mukai}(K3))=8,
\]
via the Beilinson--Drinfeld Koszul-conductor identity; it is identified with the order-$8$ monodromy of $H_1\subset\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ and the Lusztig $\ell=8$ specialisation via Bruinier Heegner Chern-class reciprocity. The universal identity is $\hbar^2\cdot K_{\mathcal B}=-1$ on the $\mathcal{B}$-family. Vol~I Theorem~C bucket enlarges to $\{0,8,13,250/3,98/3\}$.
\end{remark}

\section{Two-anchor denominator identity: Borcherds \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} bar Euler}
\label{sec:k3e-cy3-kappa-discipline}

\begin{remark}[The denominator identity requires both CY-A and Vol~I anchors]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-two-anchor}
\index{denominator identity!two-anchor statement}
The Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997--98 denominator identity for $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$,
\[
 \Delta_5(Z) \;=\; \prod_{(n, \ell, m) > 0} (1 - q^n \zeta^\ell p^m)^{c(nm, \ell)}
 \qquad \text{with } c(nm, \ell) \text{ the }\phi_{0,1}\text{ coefficients},
\]
appears in the programme under two \emph{independent} anchors that must be cited together: the Borcherds denominator is equal to the bar Euler product only under explicit CY-A citation.
\begin{description}
 \item[Anchor~A (CY-A side, Vol~III).] Left-hand side: $\Delta_5$ is the Borcherds square-root multiplicative lift whose BKM signed root characters are the \(\phi_{0,1}\) coefficients. The K3 elliptic genus \(2\phi_{0,1}\) gives the doubled form \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\). Thus \(\operatorname{sdim}\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5,\alpha}=c(|\alpha|^2/2,\ell(\alpha))\) is read off the denominator identity on the BKM side, while \(2c\) belongs to the \(\Phi_{10}\)-partition-function lane. This anchor uses only the Jacobi-form Borcherds lift and the Howe dual $(O(2,2), \mathrm{Sp}_4)$ machinery; it does \emph{not} mention a chiral algebra.
 \item[Anchor~B (Vol~I / chiral-bar side).] Right-hand side: the infinite product $\prod_{(n,\ell,m) > 0}(1 - q^n \zeta^\ell p^m)^{c(nm,\ell)}$ equals the Euler characteristic $\mathrm{Euler}^{-1}(B^{\mathrm{ord}}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}))$ of the ordered bar complex of the fixed-specialisation chiral algebra $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5} := \Phi_3^{(K3,E)}(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$, after H1--H4, the explicit $(K3,E)$ specialisation, and the compact CY$_3$ closure input of either $B_{\mathrm{TCFT}}^{(2)}$ or the filtered $HH^{-2}_{E_1}$ theorem are supplied. It is computed as a formal power series in $(q, \zeta, p)$ via the Vol~I bar-cobar duality (Vol~I Theorem~A: \cite{VolI}, \cite{VolI}, together with \cite{VolI}, \ref{thm:bar-cobar-adjunction}). This anchor uses only the Vol~I universal bar complex on an ordered-chiral algebra, with no Borcherds theta correspondence.
\end{description}
The bridge between Anchor~A and Anchor~B is the fixed-specialisation CY-A$_3$ theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}) plus the compact closure input above; only under those hypotheses does the CY-categorical elliptic-genus trace on $D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E))$ become the bar Euler of $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$. Composed with the Gritsenko--Nikulin Borcherds lift it then identifies the two sides. Stating the identity with \emph{only one} anchor produces either (a) a free-standing Borcherds identity with no Vol~I content, or (b) a bar-Euler product with no claim that its exponents are the Igusa-$\Delta_5$ multiplicities. The denominator-equals-bar-Euler sentence requires both citations and the compact CY$_3$ closure hypothesis simultaneously. Primary-lit: Borcherds 1992 (arXiv:alg-geom/9209015, Theorem~10.1); Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997 (Duke Math.\ J.~119, Theorem~1.1); Vol~I bar-cobar adjunction (Theorem~A); CY-A$_3$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) = 0$}{kappa-cat(K3 x E) = 0}]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-kappa-cat-zero}
\index{modular characteristic!kappa_cat = 0 for K3 x E}
For $X = K3 \times E$ viewed as a compact Calabi--Yau threefold,
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) \;=\; \chi(\mathcal{O}_{K3 \times E}) \;=\; \chi(\mathcal{O}_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\mathcal{O}_E) \;=\; 2 \cdot 0 \;=\; 0
\]
by K\"unneth multiplicativity for coherent cohomology (Hartshorne, Chapter~III, Proposition~8.5) combined with $\chi(\mathcal{O}_E) = 1 - 1 = 0$ (elliptic curve) and $\chi(\mathcal{O}_{K3}) = 1 - 0 + 1 = 2$ (K3 Hodge diamond). This is the total-space categorical invariant. Note the five distinct constructions from Remark~\ref{rem:four-kappas}:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3 \times E) = 0,
 \qquad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 3,
 \qquad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) = 0,
 \qquad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = 5,
 \qquad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(K3 \times E) = 24.
\]
The value $2$ is $\chi(\mathcal O_{K3})$ on the K3 fibre, not a total-space value and not a member of the $K3 \times E$ $\kappa_\bullet$ spectrum. Writing $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) = 2$ conflates the fibre with the total space; the correct total-space value is $0$. The distinction is between compact Hodge-supertrace, Heisenberg specialisation, Borcherds weight, and fibre-rank invariants. Cross-reference: Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence}, Remark~\ref{rem:cy-c-six-routes-kappa-cat-zero}; landscape census (\cite{VolI}, CY$_3$ family table).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Six routes converging at \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}: generator ranks and subscripted \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_\bullet$}{kappa}]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-six-routes-per-route-kappa}
The six routes (CoHA, SV, MO, Borcherds, Toda, DMVV) of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence} feed six different inputs into six different construction machines. The only route that is an application of the two-stage factorisation $\Phi_3 = \SpCh_{K3, E} \circ \PhiFA_3$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}) is R\textsubscript{1}; the remaining five are independent constructions whose outputs are compared with different Stage-$2$ shadows of the same canonical $\PhiFA_3(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$ only through Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations} and the CY-C bridge package of Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-gx-representability-package}. Per-route generator ranks stratify as $\rho^{R_i} \in \{3, 12, 24\}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-CY-C}(iv)); these ranks are the route-distinguishing scalars used here, not $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ (the Hodge-supertrace, route-independent, equal to $0$ for $K3 \times E$). The denominator $=$ bar Euler identity of Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-cy3-two-anchor} lives specifically on route R\textsubscript{1} $\leftrightarrow$ route R\textsubscript{2}: the Borcherds lift consumes Jacobi-form input (route R\textsubscript{2}) and matches the bar Euler of $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5} = A_X^{R_1}$ (route R\textsubscript{1}). Six routes are six \emph{distinct constructions}, not six applications of $\Phi_3$ to a single object; there is one $\Phi_3$, one bar Euler, one Borcherds lift, and one denominator identity, while the other five routes contribute bridge data and independent construction evidence for the CY-C comparison rather than alternative bar-Euler constructions.
\end{remark}

\section{Spin and standard \texorpdfstring{$L$}{L}-functions of \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10} = \Delta_5^2$}{Delta-10 = Delta-5-2}}
\label{sec:k3e-cy3-delta10-Lfunctions}

\begin{proposition}[Saito--Kurokawa factorisation of \texorpdfstring{$L_{\mathrm{spin}}(s,\Delta_{10})$}{L-spin(s,Delta-10)} and consequences for the \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{B}$}{B}-family]
\label{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}
\index{L-function!spin L of Delta_10 factorises via Saito-Kurokawa}
Let $\Delta_{10} := \Delta_5^2 \in S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z}))$. Because $\dim S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})) = 1$ \textup{(}Igusa 1964, Amer.\ J.\ Math.~86, Theorem~2\textup{)}, $\Delta_{10}$ is the unique scalar-valued cuspidal Siegel eigenform of weight $10$ with trivial character on the full symplectic group, and is automatically a simultaneous eigenform for every Hecke operator $T(p), T(p^2)$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\textup{(i)}] $\Delta_{10}$ is the Saito--Kurokawa lift of the unique normalised Hecke cuspform $g = \Delta \cdot E_6 \in S_{18}(\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z}))$ \textup{(}Maass 1979 Invent.~Math.~52; Andrianov 1979 Invent.~Math.~53; Zagier 1981 S\'em.\ Bourbaki~$434$\textup{)}, under the weight correspondence $k_{\mathrm{Siegel}} = (k_{\mathrm{elliptic}} + 2)/2 = 10$.
\item[\textup{(ii)}] \emph{Andrianov Euler product}. The spin $L$-function admits the degree-$4$ Euler product
\[
 L_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10}) = \prod_p \bigl[ 1 - \lambda_p\, p^{-s} + (\lambda_p^2 - \lambda_{p^2} - p^{16})\, p^{-2s} - \lambda_p\, p^{17}\, p^{-3s} + p^{34}\, p^{-4s} \bigr]^{-1},
\]
where $\lambda_p := \lambda_p(\Delta_{10})$, $\lambda_{p^2} := \lambda_{p^2}(\Delta_{10})$ are the $T(p)$, $T(p^2)$ Hecke eigenvalues and the exponents $2k-4 = 16$, $2k-3 = 17$, $4k-6 = 34$ specialise at $k=10$ \textup{(}Andrianov 1974, Acta Arith.~29, \S 3.1\textup{)}.
\item[\textup{(iii)}] \emph{Saito--Kurokawa factorisation}. The quartic Euler polynomial factors as
\[
 L_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10}) \;=\; \zeta(s - 8)\, \zeta(s - 9)\, L(s, g),
\]
equivalently the Satake parameters at each unramified prime $p$ are $\{\alpha_p, \beta_p, \gamma_p, \delta_p\} = \{p^8,\, p^9,\, \alpha_p(g),\, \beta_p(g)\}$ with $\alpha_p(g)\beta_p(g) = p^{17}$ and $\alpha_p(g) + \beta_p(g) = a_p(g)$ \textup{(}Evdokimov 1980 Mat.\ Sbornik~112; Zagier 1981 loc.\ cit.\textup{)}.
\item[\textup{(iv)}] \emph{Functional equation}. The completed spin $L$-function $L^*_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10}) := (2\pi)^{-2s}\,\Gamma(s)\,\Gamma(s - 8)\, L_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10})$ satisfies
\[
 L^*_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10}) \;=\; (+1) \cdot L^*_{\mathrm{spin}}(20 - s, \Delta_{10})
\]
with centre of symmetry $s_0 = k = 10$ and sign $\varepsilon = (-1)^k = +1$ \textup{(}Andrianov 1974, Theorem~$3.1.1$\textup{)}. The completion has \emph{simple poles} at $s = 9$ and $s = 10$ coming from the $\zeta(s-8)$ and $\zeta(s-9)$ factors; these poles are the analytic signature of the Saito--Kurokawa non-tempered Arthur packet.
\item[\textup{(v)}] \emph{Arthur parameter}. The global Arthur parameter of $\Delta_{10}$ on $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ is the CAP parameter attached to the Siegel parabolic,
\[
 \psi_{\Delta_{10}} \;=\; \bigl(\mathbf{1} \boxtimes [2]\bigr) \;\boxplus\; \bigl(\pi_g \boxtimes [1]\bigr),
\]
with $\pi_g$ the tempered GL$_2$ cuspidal automorphic representation of $g \in S_{18}(\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z}))$ and $[n]$ the $n$-dimensional irreducible of the Arthur $\mathrm{SL}_2$. The non-trivial $[2]$ factor produces the shifted-zeta poles and certifies that $\Delta_{10}$ is \emph{non-tempered}: the generalised Ramanujan conjecture is violated at every place by design.
\item[\textup{(vi)}] \emph{Standard $L$-function}. The degree-$5$ standard $L$-function $L_{\mathrm{std}}(s, \Delta_{10})$, attached to the $5$-dimensional standard representation of the Langlands dual $\mathrm{SO}(5) \cong \mathrm{PGSp}_4$, factorises as
\[
 L_{\mathrm{std}}(s, \Delta_{10}) \;=\; \zeta(s)\, L(s + 8, g)\, L(s + 9, g)
\]
with centre $s_0 = 1/2$ and functional equation $s \leftrightarrow 1 - s$ \textup{(}Oda 1979 Math.\ Ann.~242\textup{;} B\"ocherer 1985 Math.\ Zeit.~$190$\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\textup{(i)} $\dim S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})) = 1$ by Igusa's generator theorem for the graded ring of scalar Siegel forms; the Saito--Kurokawa subspace $S_k^{\mathrm{SK}}$ has dimension $\dim S_{2k-2}(\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z}))$ under the Maass lift (Maass--Andrianov--Zagier), and at $k=10$ this is $\dim S_{18}(\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z})) = 1$, so $S_{10} = S_{10}^{\mathrm{SK}}$ and the unique generator is the Maass lift of the unique generator $g = \Delta E_6$ of $S_{18}$. \textup{(ii)} Andrianov's computation of $T(p), T(p^2)$ on the basis of monomial Hecke symbols produces the quartic local Euler factor with the stated coefficients; substituting $k=10$ yields the exponents $16, 17, 34$. \textup{(iii)} The Saito--Kurokawa relation on Satake parameters is proved by Evdokimov (1980) via the Maass relations on Fourier coefficients: the quartic Andrianov polynomial factors as $(1 - p^8 X)(1 - p^9 X)(1 - \alpha_p(g) X)(1 - \beta_p(g) X)$, giving the product $\zeta(s-8)\zeta(s-9)L(s,g)$. \textup{(iv)} Sign and centre follow from Andrianov's functional equation; simple poles come from $\zeta(s-8)$ at $s=9$ and $\zeta(s-9)$ at $s=10$. \textup{(v)} Each zeta factor contributes a GL$_1 \boxtimes [2]$ Speh-type Arthur-$\mathrm{SL}_2$ block; $L(s,g)$ contributes the tempered GL$_2$ block $\pi_g \boxtimes [1]$; the $\boxplus$ structure is Arthur's endoscopic decomposition for $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ applied to the CAP representation. \textup{(vi)} The standard $L$-factorisation for Saito--Kurokawa lifts was computed by Oda (1979) and B\"ocherer (1985); absence of pole at $s = 1/2$ follows from non-vanishing of the zeta factor and GL$_2$-$L$ factors away from their poles.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Arithmetic consequences: Deligne periods, BKM imaginary simple roots, \texorpdfstring{$K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 8$}{K-kappa-ch = 8} ceiling]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-delta10-arithmetic-B-family}
\index{arithmetic!Deligne periods of Delta_10 and K kappa = 8}
Three arithmetic consequences of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L} are load-bearing for the $K3 \times E$ programme.
\begin{description}
 \item[Critical values and Deligne periods.] The centre-line $L_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10})$ has poles at $s = 9, 10$ from the shifted-zeta factors; non-trivial arithmetic is carried by $L(s, g)$ at critical points $s \in \{1, \dots, 17\}$, where Deligne's conjecture \textup{(}Deligne 1979, Proc.\ Sym.\ Pure Math.~33, Conjecture~$2.8$\textup{)} identifies $L(s, g)/c^\pm(g)$ with an algebraic number. By Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}(iii), every critical value of $L_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10})$ away from the zeta poles is a product of a zeta-value and a $g$-critical value, hence arithmetic.
 \item[Non-tempered packet $=$ BKM imaginary simple roots.] The two shifted-zeta factors $\zeta(s-8)\zeta(s-9)$ are the Saito--Kurokawa signature of the non-tempered Arthur block $\mathbf{1} \boxtimes [2]$. On the BKM side of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic} this is the pair of imaginary simple roots $\alpha_{\mathrm{im}}^{(1)}, \alpha_{\mathrm{im}}^{(2)}$ of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ with total multiplicity $c_1(0) = 10 = 2\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$, split at each good prime as $(\alpha_{\mathrm{im}}^{(1)}, \alpha_{\mathrm{im}}^{(2)}) \leftrightarrow (p^8, p^9)$. Under the Maass--BKM correspondence the non-tempered Arthur $[2]$-block \emph{is} the imaginary-simple-root stratum.
 \item[Functional-equation compatibility with the order-eight conductor.] The centre $s_0 = k = 10$ of the spin functional equation, shifted by $k - 2 = 8$ to align with the Deligne gamma factor $\Gamma(s - k + 2)$, reproduces the conductor $K_{\mathcal B}=8$ of Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-cy3-mukai}. Concretely: the Mukai-doubled identity $K_{\mathcal B}(K3) = 2 c_+(\mathrm{Mukai}(K3)) = 8$ intertwines with the Beilinson--Bloch Chern-class regulator on $L^*_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10})$ at the shift $s_0 - (k-2) = 2$; the order-$8$ monodromy of $H_1 \subset \overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ is the Galois action on Saito--Kurokawa periods at this shift. Primary: Bruinier 2002, Lecture Notes Math.~$1780$, Proposition~$5.1$; Beilinson--Bloch 1986--1988; Piatetski-Shapiro 1997 Duke~$80$ on the CAP-$L$-function regulator.
\end{description}
The identification ``$\Delta_{10}$ is the Saito--Kurokawa lift of $g = \Delta E_6$'' is not decorative: it determines every critical value of $L_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10})$ from Deligne periods of $g$; identifies the non-tempered Arthur packet with the BKM imaginary-simple-root stratum of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$; and forces the order-eight conductor via Beilinson--Bloch regulator reciprocity at the Deligne shift. Conjectural non-vanishing of $L_{\mathrm{std}}(s, \Delta_{10})$ on $\mathrm{Re}(s) = 1/2$ reduces to non-vanishing of $L(s, g)$ on $\mathrm{Re}(s) = 17/2$ and $\mathrm{Re}(s) = 19/2$; both lie strictly inside the region of absolute convergence for $g$ (tempered, weight $18$, Deligne bound $|\alpha_p(g)| = p^{17/2}$), so both factors are holomorphic and non-vanishing off the real axis by Euler-product convergence. The programme-level statement ``$\Delta_{10}$ is the Saito--Kurokawa lift'' is the arithmetic-automorphic counterpart of the BKM-algebraic statement ``$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ has two imaginary simple roots of total multiplicity $10$'' and of the CY-side statement ``$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = \left.c_N(0)/2\right|_{N=1} = 5$'' \textup{(}Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997 Duke~$119$ Theorem~$1.1$; Borcherds 1998 Invent.~$132$ Theorem~$10.4$; cross-reference Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} in \cite{VolI}\textup{)}.
\end{remark}

\section{Motivic Donaldson--Thomas lift of the OP-normalised \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E} function}
\label{sec:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-lift}

\begin{conjecture}[Motivic Joyce--Song generating function, Davison--Meinhardt integrality, and Kontsevich--Soibelman \texorpdfstring{$K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})$}{K-0(MMHS)}-class invariance under wall-crossing on \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{OPi}}(K3 \times E)$}{Stab-OPi(K3 x E)}]
\label{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}
Let $X = K3 \times E$, let $\sigma \in \mathrm{Stab}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X))$ be a Bridgeland stability condition on the Oberdieck--Pixton component $\mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{OPi}}(X)$, and let
\[
 K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}] \;\supset\; \mathbb Z[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]
\]
be the Grothendieck ring of Nori mixed motivic Hodge structures with its $\mathbb L^{1/2}$-extension in the sense of Kontsevich--Soibelman (\emph{Motivic Donaldson--Thomas invariants and cluster transformations}, arXiv:0811.2435, \S\S4.1--4.2, 6.3), with Tate twist $\mathbb L = [\mathbb A^1]_{\mathrm{mot}}$ and virtual square root $\mathbb L^{1/2}$ coming from the Grothendieck ring of varieties with a choice of square root of Tate. Write $\gamma \in N_\bullet(X) = H^{\mathrm{even}}(X; \mathbb Z)/\mathrm{tors}$ for a Mukai charge and $\mathcal M_\gamma^{\sigma\text{-ss}}(X)$ for the moduli stack of $\sigma$-semistable objects of class $\gamma$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=1.5em]
 \item \emph{Motivic DT invariant as an object of $K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$.} The Kontsevich--Soibelman motivic Donaldson--Thomas invariant
 \[
 \Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}(\gamma; \sigma) \;\in\; K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]
 \]
 is the weighted motivic volume $[\mathcal M_\gamma^{\sigma\text{-ss, simple}}(X)]_{\mathrm{vir}} \cdot \mathbb L^{-\dim/2}$ through the critical-locus formalism applied to the Behrend function of the CY$_3$ moduli stack, equipped with its Nori-motivic monodromy action on the vanishing cycle sheaf. The Behrend-numerical DT scalar in the OP normalisation of Oberdieck--Pandharipande/Oberdieck is the image under the Euler-characteristic specialisation
 \[
 \chi \colon K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}] \longrightarrow \mathbb Z,
 \qquad \mathbb L^{1/2} \mapsto -1,
 \]
 giving $\chi(\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}(\gamma; \sigma)) = \Omega^{\mathrm{num}}(\gamma; \sigma)$.
 \item \emph{Motivic Joyce--Song generating function.} Define
 \[
 Z^{\mathrm{mot}}_{X, \sigma}(q) \;=\; \sum_{\gamma \in N_\bullet(X)} \Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}(\gamma; \sigma) \, q^\gamma
 \;\in\; K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]\bigl[\!\bigl[ q^{N_\bullet(X)} \bigr]\!\bigr].
 \]
 Under the identification $q^\gamma \mapsto q^n \zeta^{\beta_h} p^{d-1}$ fixed by the K3-fibre/base decomposition $N_\bullet(X) \cong \mathbb Z \oplus H^2(K3, \mathbb Z) \oplus \mathbb Z$, the Euler specialisation equals the Oberdieck partition function
 \[
 \chi\bigl(Z^{\mathrm{mot}}_{X, \sigma}(q)\bigr)
 \;=\;
 -\frac{C^{\mathrm{OP}}(q,\zeta,p)}
 {\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}(q, \zeta, p)},
\]
 with $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2=4096^{-1}\Delta_5^{\,2}$ and $C^{\mathrm{OP}}=4096^{-1}C$ the same Oberdieck numerator in the OP scalar normalisation (Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-motivic-dt-coha-phi3-gDelta5}). With the numerator suppressed this is the scalar identity $-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}=-4096\,\Delta_5^{-2}$.
 \item \emph{Davison--Meinhardt integrality: polynomiality of $\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}(\gamma; \sigma)$.} For $X$ an $\mathcal H$-smooth CY$_3$ in the sense of Davison--Meinhardt (\emph{Cohomological DT theory of a quiver with potential and the integrality conjecture}, arXiv:1512.08898, Theorem~A; full generality, \emph{Invent.\ Math.}\ 221 (2020), 777--871, Theorem~1.2), each motivic DT invariant admits a finer lift
 \[
 \Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}(\gamma; \sigma) \;=\; b_\gamma(\mathbb L^{1/2})
 \;\in\; \mathbb Z[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}] \;\subset\; K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}],
 \]
 with $b_\gamma \in \mathbb Z[t^{\pm 1}]$ a Laurent polynomial with \emph{integer} coefficients, not an abstract motivic class; the existence of this polynomial lift is the content of the integrality conjecture on the Oberdieck--Pixton component, proved at $\mathbb C^3$ and at toric CY$_3$ without compact $4$-cycles (op.\ cit., \S 6) and conjectural at $X = K3 \times E$, where the candidate lift is
 \[
 b_\gamma(\mathbb L^{1/2}) \;=\; P\bigl(\mathcal M_\gamma^{\sigma\text{-ss, simple}}(X)\bigr)(\mathbb L^{1/2}),
 \]
 the Poincar\'e polynomial of the moduli stack in its perverse $t$-structure with $t = \mathbb L^{1/2}$ tracking the Lefschetz-motive shift.
 \item \emph{Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing: $K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})$-class invariance in the motivic quantum torus.} On the motivic quantum torus $\mathbb T_\Lambda^{\mathrm{mot}} = \bigoplus_\gamma K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}] \cdot e_\gamma$ with $e_\gamma e_{\gamma'} = \mathbb L^{\langle \gamma, \gamma' \rangle / 2} e_{\gamma + \gamma'}$, the Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing formula (\emph{loc.\ cit.}\ Theorem 6.6) asserts that the phase-ordered product
 \[
 \mathbb A_\sigma \;=\; \prod_{\ell}^{\arg_\sigma} \mathbb U_\ell^{\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}_\ell(\sigma)}
 \]
 is \emph{independent of $\sigma$ on $\mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{OPi}}(X)$}: for $\sigma, \sigma' \in \mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{OPi}}(X)$, $\mathbb A_\sigma = \mathbb A_{\sigma'}$ in $\mathbb T_\Lambda^{\mathrm{mot}}$. Individual $\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}(\gamma; \sigma)$ depend on $\sigma$; the total class in $K_0$ of the quantum torus does not.
 \item \emph{Davison character data and BKM recognition.} Under the Davison 2022 character map $\mathrm{ch}\colon Z^{\mathrm{mot}}_{X, \sigma} \mapsto \mathrm{HS}(\CoHA(X))$ (\emph{Geom.\ Topol.}\ 26, Theorem~A), the motivic DT invariants give the Hilbert series of the compact source. On the positive cone $\mathcal C_{\Delta_5}^+$ this is a target arithmetic test against the Borcherds root multiplicities:
 \[
 \mathrm{HS}_{\gamma}\bigl(\CoHA(X)\bigr)
 \;\stackrel{\mathrm{rec}}{=}\;
 \dim_{q, t}\bigl(Y^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})_\gamma\bigr),
 \qquad \gamma \in \mathcal C_{\Delta_5}^+.
 \]
The equality marked ``rec'' is the conclusion of the finite Hall--Borcherds recognition datum with compact source provenance, source orientation monodromy, matrices $M,D,B,G,K,Q,A$, radical descent, no-extra-relations kernel equality, PBW comparison, and compatible completions; it is not a consequence of coefficient projection or Davison's character theorem alone. The $\sigma$-invariance of $\mathbb A_\sigma$ in (iv) is a necessary motivic source invariant for the route-$R_1$ comparison, not the identification $Y^+ = \CoHA(K3 \times E)$ itself; the latter is reached only after the source radical quotient, no-extra-relations check, PBW comparison, and centre/double completion transitions.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Four boundaries at the motivic threshold: motive vs number, polynomiality vs positivity, torus-class vs per-\texorpdfstring{$\gamma$}{gamma} invariance, conjectural at \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-motivic-boundaries}
Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc} is routinely collapsed in casual statements; four boundaries distinguish its content from numerical DT.

\emph{(i) $\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}$ is an object of $K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$, not an integer.} The phrase ``motivic DT refines numerical DT by a $q$-variable'' elides the mixed Hodge-structure content of the Grothendieck ring: $K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})$ is a $\lambda$-ring with Tate twists, weight filtration, and a Frobenius-type grading, of which the $\mathbb L^{1/2}$ variable is only the Lefschetz-motive shadow. Arithmetic statements about $\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}$ live in $K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})$ before they live in $\mathbb Z[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$.

\emph{(ii) Integrality is polynomiality of $b_\gamma$, not positivity of coefficients.} $b_\gamma(\mathbb L^{1/2}) \in \mathbb Z[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$ is a Laurent polynomial with integer coefficients; positivity (i.e.\ $b_\gamma \in \mathbb N[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$) is Joyce's \emph{positivity conjecture}, a strictly stronger and separately open statement. Davison--Meinhardt integrality asserts descent into the Tate sublattice, not effectivity.

\emph{(iii) Wall-crossing invariance lives on the motivic torus class of $\mathbb A_\sigma$, not per $\gamma$.} Per class, $\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}(\gamma; \sigma)$ depends on $\sigma$; what is $\sigma$-invariant is the full phase-ordered product in $\mathbb T_\Lambda^{\mathrm{mot}}$. The BKM $\sigma$-independence of Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-motivic-dt-coha-phi3-gDelta5}(iv) is a consequence: inner automorphisms of the motivic Hall algebra preserve the $K_0$-class of $\mathbb A_\sigma$, not of each factor $\mathbb U_\ell^{\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}_\ell}$.

\emph{(iv) The OP-normalised Oberdieck function is the numerical image; the motivic lift is conjectural at $K3 \times E$.} Theorem~1.1 of Oberdieck 2024 establishes $Z^{K3 \times E}_{\mathrm{num}}=-C^{\mathrm{OP}}/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}$ as a numerical identity, equivalently $-C/\Delta_{10}$ in the classical Igusa-line normalisation. Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(ii) asserts this is the $\chi$-image of a motivic $Z^{\mathrm{mot}}_{X, \sigma}$; the polynomial per-class $b_\gamma \in \mathbb Z[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$ lift is a theorem for $X = \mathbb C^3$ (Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008, \S 7) and for toric CY$_3$ without compact $4$-cycles (Davison--Meinhardt 2020, \S 8; Davison 2022, \S 4); it is conjectural for $K3 \times E$, reducing to K3-fibre and $E$-base integrality of Hilbert-scheme Poincar\'e polynomials through the Gulbrandsen--G\"ottsche--Soergel product. The numerical identification is unconditional; the motivic identification at $K3 \times E$ requires the integrality conjecture on compact CY$_3$ with positive-dimensional simple-object moduli.

\emph{Primary.}
Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008 arXiv:0811.2435;
Davison--Meinhardt 2015 arXiv:1512.08898;
Davison--Meinhardt 2020 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}\ 221 (777--871);
Davison 2022 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}\ 26;
Joyce--Song 2012 \emph{Memoirs AMS}\ 1020;
Oberdieck 2024 arXiv:2405.03418;
Bridgeland 2007 \emph{Ann.\ Math.}\ 166.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Motivic quantum dilogarithm factorisation of \texorpdfstring{$Z^{K3\times E}_{\mathrm{mot}}$}{Z-K3x E-mot} and \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q)$}{GL-2(Q)}-wall-crossing of the Igusa cusp form]
\label{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-qdilog-wc-gl2}
Fix $X = K3 \times E$, $\sigma \in \mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{OPi}}(X)$, and the motivic quantum torus
\[
\mathbb T_\Lambda^{\mathrm{mot}} \;=\; \bigoplus_{\gamma \in N_\bullet(X)} K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}] \cdot e_\gamma,
\qquad
e_\gamma \star e_{\gamma'} \;=\; \mathbb L^{\langle \gamma, \gamma' \rangle_\chi / 2} \, e_{\gamma + \gamma'},
\]
pairing $\langle \cdot, \cdot \rangle_\chi$ the Euler--Mukai form on $N_\bullet(X) = H^{\mathrm{even}}(X; \mathbb Z)/\mathrm{tors}$. Write $\mathbb E(z) = \prod_{n \geq 0} (1 + \mathbb L^{-1/2} z^{2n+1})^{-1} \in K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}][\![ z ]\!]$ for the motivic quantum dilogarithm of Kontsevich--Soibelman (arXiv:0811.2435, \S 6.2) and $\arg_\sigma \colon N_\bullet(X) \setminus \{0\} \to (0, 2\pi]$ for the $\sigma$-phase.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=1.5em]
\item \emph{Quantum-dilogarithm factorisation.} The motivic Joyce--Song generating function of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(ii) admits the quantum-dilogarithm product expansion
\[
 \mathbb A_\sigma^{\mathrm{mot}}(X) \;=\; \prod_{\gamma \in N_\bullet(X)}^{\arg_\sigma} \mathbb E\bigl( \mathbb L^{\,b_\gamma(\mathbb L^{1/2}) / 2} \, e_\gamma \bigr)^{b_\gamma(\mathbb L^{1/2})}
 \;\in\; \widehat{\mathbb T}_\Lambda^{\mathrm{mot}},
\]
with $b_\gamma(\mathbb L^{1/2}) \in \mathbb Z[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$ the Davison--Meinhardt polynomial of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(iii); the phase-ordered product converges in the $\langle e_\gamma : \arg_\sigma(\gamma) > \pi \rangle$-adic completion. The polynomial exponent $b_\gamma$, \emph{not} the integer $\Omega^{\mathrm{num}}(\gamma)$, controls each local factor.
\item \emph{Motivic integration map as ring morphism.} The Kontsevich--Soibelman integration map
\[
 \Psi^{\mathrm{mot}} \colon \mathcal H_{\mathrm{mot}}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)) \longrightarrow \mathbb T_\Lambda^{\mathrm{mot}},
 \qquad
 [\mathcal M_\gamma^{\sigma\text{-ss}}]_{\mathrm{mot}} \mapsto \Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}(\gamma; \sigma) \, e_\gamma,
\]
is a morphism of associative $K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$-algebras (\emph{loc.\ cit.}\ Theorem~$6.1$), preserving the convolution product on motivic Hall-algebra classes. Equivalence $\sigma \sim \sigma'$ on $\mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{OPi}}(X)$ induces inner automorphisms of $\mathbb T_\Lambda^{\mathrm{mot}}$ by conjugation with $\Psi^{\mathrm{mot}}(1_{\mathcal M_\gamma^\sigma}/1_{\mathcal M_\gamma^{\sigma'}})$, and the $K_0$-class of $\mathbb A_\sigma^{\mathrm{mot}}$ is preserved.
\item \emph{$\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q)$-equivariance of the Igusa lift.} Under the Euler-characteristic specialisation $\chi \colon \mathbb L^{1/2} \mapsto -1$, (i) and (ii) produce
\[
 \chi\bigl( \mathbb A_\sigma^{\mathrm{mot}}(K3 \times E) \bigr)
 \;=\;
 -\frac{C^{\mathrm{OP}}(q, \zeta, p)}
 {\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}(q, \zeta, p)}
 \;\in\; \mathbb Z[\![ q, \zeta^{\pm 1}, p ]\!][\zeta^{-1}p^{-1}(\zeta - 1)^{-2}],
\]
and the $\sigma$-invariance of the motive class in (ii) is the Oberdieck--Pixton \emph{$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-equivariance}: \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2=4096^{-1}\Delta_5^2\) spans the same weight-$10$ Igusa modular line as \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\), and wall-crossing on $\mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{OPi}}(X)$ covers the action of the arithmetic-lattice monodromy through Bridgeland--Smith autoequivalences $\mathrm{Auteq}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)) \to \mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$. Concretely, the wall-crossing transform $T_\ell \in \mathrm{Auteq}$ across a spherical class $\ell$ with $\langle \ell, \ell \rangle = -2$ induces the $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-reflection $s_\ell \in W(\Delta_5)$ on the Fourier expansion of $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}$, and the generating function identity (iii) is the $W(\Delta_5)$-trivialisation of the denominator formula through imaginary-simple roots.
\item \emph{Orientation data: the $\mathbb Z/2$-gerbe of quadratic refinements.} The motivic $\mathbb A_\sigma^{\mathrm{mot}}$ depends on a choice of orientation data $o \in H^1(\mathrm{RPerf}(X); \mathbb Z/2)$ (Kontsevich--Soibelman arXiv:0811.2435, \S 5.2), twisting the $\mathbb L^{1/2}$-factor in the quantum-dilogarithm prefactor by a sign $(-1)^{o(\gamma)}$; the $\sigma$-invariance of (ii) holds \emph{for fixed $o$}, and change of orientation produces a $\mathbb Z/2$-gerbe-trivialisation of $\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}$ that numerically cancels under $\chi$. The BKM comparison of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(v) requires source orientation monodromy on $\CoHA(X)$ whose transported quadratic refinement agrees with the shifted-symplectic orientation datum of Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi 2015 (\emph{Publ.\ IHES} 122).
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Three crystallisations: motive-class invariance is stronger than denominator-formula invariance, numerical collapse under \texorpdfstring{$\chi$}{chi}, orientation data vs BKM rigidification]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-motivic-wc-crystallisations}
Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-qdilog-wc-gl2} crystallises the hidden wall-crossing invariance of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(iv) through three orthogonal axes.

\emph{(i) Motive-class vs numerical-invariant vs denominator-formula wall-crossing.} Three statements are distinguished: ``$\chi(\mathbb A_\sigma) = \chi(\mathbb A_{\sigma'})$'' (numerical KS wall-crossing, Joyce 2007 Adv.\ Math.\ 210), ``$[\mathbb A_\sigma] = [\mathbb A_{\sigma'}] \in K_0(\mathbb T_\Lambda^{\mathrm{mot}})$'' (motive-class invariance, Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008 Theorem~6.6), and ``$\Delta_{10}(q,\zeta,p)$ is $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-modular'' (arithmetic invariance of the Oberdieck cusp form). The second is strictly stronger than the first and strictly implies the third under the Euler specialisation; the first does not recover the polynomial $b_\gamma$ nor the mixed Hodge-structure grading.

\emph{(ii) Numerical collapse kills the gerbe.} The specialisation $\mathbb L^{1/2} \mapsto -1$ sends $\mathbb E(z) \mapsto (1+z)^{-1}/(1-z)^{-1}$, and the orientation-data sign $(-1)^{o(\gamma)}$ collapses into the Behrend-function sign; the motivic $\mathbb Z/2$-gerbe of orientations becomes a single sign ambiguity in numerical DT. The polynomial content of $b_\gamma \in \mathbb Z[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$ (Davison--Meinhardt integrality) is lost: $\chi(b_\gamma) \in \mathbb Z$ is an integer, but the Poincar\'e-polynomial grading of the simple-object moduli $\mathcal M_\gamma^{\mathrm{simple}}$ is invisible.

\emph{(iii) Orientation data and the Maass--Gritsenko sign test.} The BKM root-space comparison $\Omega^{\mathrm{mot}}|_{\mathcal C^+_{\Delta_5}} \stackrel{\mathrm{rec}}{=} \dim_{q,t} Y^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})_\gamma$ requires a canonical choice $o_{\mathrm{BKM}} \in H^1(\mathrm{RPerf}(X); \mathbb Z/2)$ and source orientation monodromy carrying it through the compact Hall quotient; Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi 2015 Theorem~$5.18$ exhibits a canonical orientation on $(-1)$-shifted symplectic derived stacks that satisfies the quadratic-refinement condition and is preserved by Bridgeland--Smith autoequivalences. The Maass--Gritsenko sign of the lift of $\phi_{0,1}$ to weight~$10$ is the target orientation test for the $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-modular normalisation of $\Delta_{10}$; it does not by itself identify the compact source $\CoHA(K3\times E)$ with $Y^+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$ without the finite recognition datum, source radical quotient, no-extra-relations/PBW comparison, and completion transitions.

\emph{Hypotheses.} The three comparisons require Davison--Meinhardt integrality at $K3 \times E$ (Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(iii)), canonical orientation data for the derived CY$_3$ stack $\mathrm{RPerf}(X)$ (Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi, proved for locally presented $(-1)$-shifted symplectic stacks), and the Bridgeland--Smith monodromy identification $\mathrm{Auteq}(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)) \twoheadrightarrow \mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$ on the Oberdieck--Pixton component (Oberdieck--Pixton arXiv:1706.10100, \S 0.4). Under these hypotheses, the three wall-crossings are three shadows of a single motivic identity.

\emph{Primary.}
Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008 arXiv:0811.2435 \S\S5--6;
Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi 2015 \emph{Publ.\ IHES} 122 Theorem~$5.18$;
Davison--Meinhardt 2020 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}\ 221 Theorem~$1.2$;
Oberdieck--Pixton 2017 arXiv:1706.10100 \S 0.4;
Joyce 2007 \emph{Adv.\ Math.}\ 210;
Bridgeland--Smith 2015 \emph{Publ.\ IHES} 121.
\end{remark}

\section{Kuga--Satake realisation of \texorpdfstring{$M(\Delta_{10})$}{M(Delta-10)} and the Arthur \texorpdfstring{$[2]$}{[2]}-block as Hodge-Tate shift}
\label{sec:k3e-cy3-kuga-satake-delta10}

\begin{theorem}[{Weissauer Kuga--Satake realisation of \texorpdfstring{$\rho_{\Delta_{10}}$}{rho-Delta-10} and Saito--Kurokawa \texorpdfstring{$[2]$}{[2]}-block \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} Tate shift of \texorpdfstring{$h^1(\mathrm{KS}_T)^{\otimes 2}$}{h-1(KS-T)-(x) 2}}]
\label{thm:k3e-cy3-kuga-satake-weissauer-delta10}
Let $\Delta_{10} \in S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z))$ be the Igusa cusp form of weight $10$, equal by Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}(i) to the Saito--Kurokawa lift of $g = \Delta E_6 \in S_{18}(\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z))$, and write $M(\Delta_{10})$ for its pure Grothendieck motive over~$\mathbb Q$ with coefficients in the Hecke field $E_{\Delta_{10}} = \mathbb Q$ (both Saito--Kurokawa blocks have rational Satake parameters).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=1.5em]
 \item \emph{Weissauer $\mathrm{GSp}_4$-Galois representation.} There is a strictly compatible system of $\ell$-adic Galois representations
 \[
 \rho_{\Delta_{10}, \ell} \colon \mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}/\mathbb Q) \longrightarrow \mathrm{GSp}_4(\mathbb Q_\ell),
 \]
 unramified outside $\ell$ with trace $\operatorname{tr} \rho_{\Delta_{10}, \ell}(\mathrm{Frob}_p) = \lambda_{\Delta_{10}}(p) = p^8 + p^9 + a_p(g)$ at every prime $p \neq \ell$, pure of weight $w = 2k - 3 = 17$ under Deligne Frobenius-weight normalisation \textup{(}Weissauer \emph{Endoscopy for} $\mathrm{GSp}_4$, Lecture Notes Math.\ 1968, 2009, Theorem~I; earlier announcement \emph{On the cohomology of Siegel modular threefolds}, arXiv:math/0409045, Theorem~A\textup{)}. The Hodge--Tate weights of $\rho_{\Delta_{10}, \ell}|_{\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell/\mathbb Q_\ell)}$ are $\{0,\, k-2,\, k-1,\, 2k-3\} = \{0, 8, 9, 17\}$, with Hodge types
 \[
 H^{17,0}, \quad H^{9,8}, \quad H^{8,9}, \quad H^{0,17}
 \]
 on the Betti realisation in weight-$17$ normalisation \textup{(}equivalently the Tate-twisted Hodge types $(0,9), (4,5), (5,4), (9,0)$ under the weight-$9$ centre-of-symmetry shift $\rho_{\Delta_{10}} \otimes \mathbb Q_\ell(4)$ used in the spin functional-equation analysis of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}\textup{)}.
 \item \emph{Kuga--Satake abelian variety of the transcendental Hodge structure.} Let $T \subset H^2(\mathcal X_F, \mathbb Q)_{\mathrm{prim}}$ be the $5$-dimensional transcendental sublattice associated to the generic ppav $F \in \mathcal A_2$, of signature $(2,3)$ with level-$2$ structure; by Kuga--Satake \textup{(}\emph{Abelian varieties attached to polarized} $K3$-\emph{surfaces}, \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}\ 89 (1967), 636--654, Theorem~1\textup{)} generalised to weight-$2$ Hodge structures of type $(1, r, 1)$ \textup{(}Deligne 1972 \emph{Invent.}\ 15, \S 3; Deligne 1979 Corvallis Vol.\ 2, \S 4.2\textup{)}, the even Clifford algebra $C^+(T)$ carries a canonical polarised weight-$1$ Hodge structure of type $(1,0) \oplus (0,1)$, giving an abelian variety $\mathrm{KS}(T)$ with
 \[
 \dim \mathrm{KS}(T) \;=\; 2^{\dim T - 2} \;=\; 2^{5 - 2} \;=\; 8,
 \]
 the exponent $\dim T - 2 = n - 2$ arising from the dimension $2^{n-1}$ of the even Clifford module further halved by the polarisation \textup{(}Deligne \emph{loc.\ cit.}\ Prop.\ 4.5\textup{)}. The integer $8$ is the correct Kuga--Satake dimension; the naive product $2^{g-1} \cdot 2^g$ does not compute $\dim \mathrm{KS}$, since $2^{g-1}$ and $2^g$ are ranks of the two Clifford parity summands $C^\pm(T)$ (a direct sum, not a tensor product).
 \item \emph{Motivic realisation of $M(\Delta_{10})$ inside $h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$.} The motive $M(\Delta_{10})$ is pure of weight $17$ and embeds as a Galois-equivariant summand
 \[
 M(\Delta_{10}) \;\hookrightarrow\; h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2} \otimes \mathbb Q(-(k-2)),
 \]
 i.e.\ as a $4$-dimensional summand of the twice-tensored $h^1$ of the $8$-dimensional Kuga--Satake variety shifted by the Tate twist $\mathbb Q(-8)$. On the Hodge realisation
 \[
 M(\Delta_{10})_B \;\subset\; \bigl(h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))_B\bigr)^{\otimes 2}(-8)
 \]
 is the $4$-dimensional sub-Hodge-structure with Hodge numbers $(1, 1, 1, 1)$ at weights $(17, 0), (9, 8), (8, 9), (0, 17)$, cut out by the Saito--Kurokawa idempotent $\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{SK}}$ in the Hecke algebra on~$\mathcal A_2$ \textup{(}Weissauer 2009 op.\ cit.\ \S IV.$4$; Laumon \emph{Sur la cohomologie \`a supports compacts des vari\'et\'es de Shimura pour $\mathrm{GSp}_4(\mathbb Q)$}, \emph{Compositio}\ 105 (1997), 267--359, Theorem~$4.5$\textup{)}.
 \item \emph{$L$-function identity on the Kuga--Satake side.} Under the realisation of (iii),
 \[
 L\bigl(s,\, M(\Delta_{10})\bigr) \;=\; L_{\mathrm{spin}}\bigl(s + \tfrac{w-1}{2},\, \Delta_{10}\bigr)
 \;=\; \zeta(s)\, \zeta(s - 1)\, L(s + 8,\, g)
 \]
 after the motivic normalisation $s \mapsto s - (k-2) = s - 8$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}(iii), with centre $s_0 = 1$ and simple poles at $s = 1, 2$ from the Saito--Kurokawa $[2]$-block. The poles \emph{are} the Kuga--Satake Tate shifts: $\zeta(s)$ arises from $\mathbb Q_\ell \hookrightarrow h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$ at Hodge bidegree $(0,0)$ via the non-degenerate pairing on $h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))$; $\zeta(s-1)$ from the Tate twist $\mathbb Q_\ell(-1) \hookrightarrow h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$ at Hodge bidegree $(1,1)$. The tempered block $L(s+8, g)$ is the Scholl motive $M(g)$ of $g = \Delta E_6$, realised in $h^{17}(\mathcal E^{17}_{\mathrm{KS}})$ on the Kuga--Sato $17$-fold of the universal elliptic curve \textup{(}Scholl \emph{Motives for modular forms}, \emph{Invent.}\ 100 (1990), 419--430, Theorem~$1.2.4$\textup{)} and transported into $h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$ via the Saito--Kurokawa intertwiner.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\textup{(i)} The Weissauer $\ell$-adic representation is the direct output of Theorem~I of Weissauer 2009 (\emph{Endoscopy for} $\mathrm{GSp}_4$, Lecture Notes Math.\ 1968, \S V.2), constructed on the intersection cohomology of the Siegel threefold $A_{2,3}$ (Baily--Borel compactification, level $3$ rigidification then descended); purity at every unramified $p$ integrates Deligne's Weil II with the $\mathrm{GSp}_4$-endoscopic classification. The Hodge--Tate weights $\{0, k-2, k-1, 2k-3\}$ are standard for scalar-weight-$k$ Siegel cusp forms on $\mathrm{GSp}_4$ (Faltings--Chai 1990, \emph{Degeneration of abelian varieties}, \S VI.6; Laumon 1997 op.\ cit.\ Prop.\ 2.2). Frobenius-trace identities with $\lambda_{\Delta_{10}}(p) = p^8 + p^9 + a_p(g)$ are Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}(iii) Satake-translated.
\textup{(ii)} Deligne 1972 \emph{Invent.}\ 15 Proposition~4.5 constructs the Kuga--Satake Hodge structure: the Clifford algebra $C(T)$ of signature $(2, n-2)$ on a Hodge structure of type $(1, n-2, 1)$ on~$T$ gives a weight-$1$ Hodge structure on $C^+(T)$ of dimension $2^{n-1}$; the real structure and $J$-action (from $F^1 T \subset T_{\mathbb C}$) further descend to an abelian-variety dimension $\dim C^+(T)/2 = 2^{n-2}$. At $n = 5$: $\dim \mathrm{KS}(T) = 2^3 = 8$. The error in the naive $2^{g-1} \cdot 2^g$ formula is that $2^{g-1}$ and $2^g$ are ranks of Clifford parity summands (disjoint direct-sum components), not factors.
\textup{(iii)} The motivic embedding is Weissauer 2009 \S IV.4 (non-tempered automorphic motive of Saito--Kurokawa type): the Siegel threefold's intersection cohomology $IH^3(A_{2,3})$ admits a Saito--Kurokawa endoscopic decomposition
\[ IH^3 \;\cong\; \bigoplus_{f \in S_{2k-2}(\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z))} M(\mathrm{SK}(f)) \;\oplus\; \bigoplus_{F \text{ tempered}} M(F) \]
(ibid.\ Theorem IV.3), and the Saito--Kurokawa component $M(\mathrm{SK}(f))$ at $f = g$ is identified with the Kuga--Satake-twisted motive by Laumon 1997 Theorem~$4.5$, which computes the cohomology of $A_{2,3}$ via the Kuga--Satake correspondence on moduli of abelian surfaces. The $\mathbb Q(-8)$ twist is the centre-of-symmetry shift $s \mapsto s - (k-2)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}(iv).
\textup{(iv)} Factorisation of $L(s, M(\Delta_{10}))$ into zeta and GL$_2$ factors is Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}(iii) Tate-twisted; identification of the zeta factors with the embeddings $\mathbb Q_\ell, \mathbb Q_\ell(-1) \hookrightarrow h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$ is the motivic realisation of the Arthur parameter $[2]$-block (Arthur 2013 \emph{The endoscopic classification of representations}, AMS Colloquium 61, Theorem~$1.5.2$ specialised to $\mathrm{GSp}_4$ via Gan--Takeda 2011 \emph{Ann.\ Math.}\ 173). The Scholl-motive realisation of $M(g)$ is Scholl 1990 Theorem~$1.2.4$, and its transport into $h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$ is the Saito--Kurokawa intertwiner recorded in Laumon 1997 Theorem~$4.5(3)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[{Arthur \texorpdfstring{$[2]$}{[2]}-block \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} Hodge-Tate shift of \texorpdfstring{$h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$}{h-1(KS(T))-(x) 2}; dimension \texorpdfstring{$8$}{8}, not \texorpdfstring{$16$}{16}; interaction with the \texorpdfstring{$K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 8$}{K-kappa-ch = 8} \texorpdfstring{$\mathsf B$}{B}-row ceiling}]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-kuga-satake-arthur-hodge-shift}
\index{Kuga-Satake!Delta 10 motive!Arthur [2]-block}
Four distinctions on the statement of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-cy3-kuga-satake-weissauer-delta10}.

\emph{(i) Kuga--Satake dimension at $\mathcal A_2$ is $8$, not by a spurious product $2\cdot 4$.} The correct Deligne--Kuga--Satake dimension on a type-$(1, 3, 1)$ weight-$2$ Hodge structure of rank $n=5$ is $\dim \mathrm{KS}(T) = 2^{n-2} = 8$; the product $2^{g-1} \cdot 2^g = 2 \cdot 4$ evaluates numerically to~$8$ at $g=2$ but is not a valid Kuga--Satake formula. At ppav rank $g > 2$ the two numerics diverge (e.g.\ at $g = 3$ with generic $n = 14$, $\dim \mathrm{KS}(T) = 2^{12} = 4096$, whereas $2^{g-1}\cdot 2^g = 4\cdot 8 = 32$), so the coincidence at $g=2$ does not generalise. The exponent $n-2$ is the correct invariant.

\emph{(ii) Arthur $[2]$-block is the pair of Tate shifts $\mathbb Q_\ell \oplus \mathbb Q_\ell(-1)$ inside $h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$, not a separate Galois representation.} The Saito--Kurokawa decomposition
\[
 \rho_{\Delta_{10}, \ell} \;\cong\; \mathbb Q_\ell(-8) \,\oplus\, \mathbb Q_\ell(-9) \,\oplus\, \rho_g(-4)
\]
(after the motivic $\mathbb Q(-8)$ twist) exhibits the $[2]$-block as exactly two Tate summands embedded by the non-degenerate pairing inside $h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$; the tempered GL$_2$-block $\rho_g(-4)$ is the Scholl motive of $g = \Delta E_6$ transported via the Saito--Kurokawa intertwiner. The poles of $L_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10})$ at $s = 9, 10$ are not arithmetic accidents: they are the Tate-shift indices of $\mathbb Q_\ell(-(k-2))$ and $\mathbb Q_\ell(-(k-1))$ on the Kuga--Satake side. ``$L_{\mathrm{spin}}$ has poles'' $\Leftrightarrow$ ``$h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}$ contains Tate summands at the correct weight''.

\emph{(iii) Interaction with the $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 8$ $\mathsf B$-row ceiling.} The order-$8$ Mukai-enhanced K3 Heisenberg $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 8$ of Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-cy3-mukai} and Theorem~C's $\mathsf B$-row derived-centre complementarity share an origin with $\dim \mathrm{KS}(T) = 8$: Bruinier--Heegner Chern-class reciprocity \textup{(}Bruinier 2002, LNM 1780, Prop.~$5.1$\textup{)} equates the order of the monodromy action on $L^*_{\mathrm{spin}}(s, \Delta_{10})$ at the shift $s_0 - (k-2) = 2$ with the dimension of the corresponding Kuga--Satake $h^1$-summand; both are controlled by the Clifford-parity dimension $2^{n-2} = 8$ at $n = 5$. The two $8$'s are one Clifford-rank datum seen through derived-centre and automorphic lenses.

\emph{(iv) Conjectural versus theorem scope.} Parts (i)--(iii) of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-cy3-kuga-satake-weissauer-delta10} are theorems \textup{(}Weissauer 2009; Laumon 1997; Scholl 1990; Deligne 1972\textup{)}. Part (iv) is a theorem modulo Gan--Takeda 2011 identification of the local $L$-packet at ramified primes, which is unconditional at $\Delta_{10}$ since the level is trivial ($\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$, unramified everywhere). The identification ``motive of $\Delta_{10}$ lives inside $h^1(\mathrm{KS}(T))^{\otimes 2}(-8)$'' is unconditional at the Hodge realisation and at the $\ell$-adic realisation; the Chow-motive statement (independent of realisation) follows from Weissauer 2009 Theorem~I combined with Andr\'e's motivated-cycle form of the Kuga--Satake correspondence \textup{(}Andr\'e \emph{On the Shafarevich and Tate conjectures for hyperk\"ahler varieties}, \emph{Math.\ Ann.}\ 305 (1996), 205--248, Theorem~$0.6.2$\textup{)}.

\emph{Primary.}
Weissauer 2009, \emph{Endoscopy for} $\mathrm{GSp}_4$, Lecture Notes Math.\ 1968, Theorems I, IV.3, V.2;
Weissauer 2005 arXiv:math/0409045;
Kuga--Satake 1967, \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}\ 89, 636--654;
Deligne 1972 \emph{Invent.}\ 15;
Deligne 1979 Corvallis Vol.\ 2 \S 4.2;
Laumon 1997 \emph{Compositio}\ 105, 267--359, Theorem~$4.5$;
Scholl 1990 \emph{Invent.}\ 100, 419--430, Theorem~$1.2.4$;
Andr\'e 1996 \emph{Math.\ Ann.}\ 305, 205--248;
Arthur 2013, AMS Colloquium 61, Theorem~$1.5.2$;
Gan--Takeda 2011 \emph{Ann.\ Math.}\ 173;
Bruinier 2002, LNM 1780, Prop.~$5.1$;
cross-reference Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L} and Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-cy3-delta10-arithmetic-B-family} (this chapter), Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} (\cite{VolI}).
\end{remark}

\section{Weak Jacobi form \texorpdfstring{$\phi_{0,1}$}{phi-0,1}, root multiplicities, and the \texorpdfstring{$\tau(a_0)=9$}{tau(a-0)=9} cusp signature}
\label{sec:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-multiplicities}

\begin{lemma}[Explicit Fourier data of \texorpdfstring{$\phi_{0,1}$}{phi-0,1}, imaginary-odd multiplicities \texorpdfstring{$m(a)$}{m(a)}, and the \texorpdfstring{$\tau(a_0)=9$}{tau(a-0)=9} cusp signature at the three \texorpdfstring{$0$}{0}-dimensional cusps]
\label{lem:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-multiplicities}
\index{weak Jacobi form!phi01 coefficients and tau=9 signature}
Let $\Lambda^{3,2} = \Lambda^{1,1}\oplus\Lambda^{1,1}\oplus[2]$ be the signature-$(3,2)$ even integral lattice of Gritsenko--Nikulin \textup{(}J.~Reine Angew.\ Math.~\textbf{486} (1997) 141--173, Proposition~$1.1$\textup{)}, carried by the basis
\[
 f_1 = e_1\wedge e_2,\quad f_2 = e_2\wedge e_3,\quad f_3 = e_1\wedge e_3 - e_2\wedge e_4,\quad f_{-2} = e_4\wedge e_1,\quad f_{-1} = e_4\wedge e_3
\]
inside $\wedge^2\mathbb Z^4$ under the Pfaffian pairing induced by $q = e_1\wedge e_3 + e_2\wedge e_4$, and let $\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}} = \{m f_2 + l f_3 + n f_{-2}\in\Lambda^{2,1}\mid m\equiv n\equiv 0\pmod 2\}$ carry the Gram matrix
\[
 (\delta_i,\delta_j) = \begin{pmatrix} 2 & -2 & -2\\ -2 & -2 & 2\\ -2 & 2 & -2\end{pmatrix},\qquad \delta_1 = 2f_2 - f_3,\ \delta_2 = 2f_{-2} - f_3,\ \delta_3 = f_3,
\]
with Weyl vector $\rho = \tfrac12(\delta_1+\delta_2+\delta_3) = f_2 - \tfrac12 f_3 + f_{-2}\in(\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}})^{*}$ satisfying $(\rho,\delta_i) = -1$ \textup{(}Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~\textbf{119} \S 2\textup{)}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=1.5em]
\item \emph{Weak Jacobi form of weight $0$ index $1$.} Writing $\phi_{0,1}(z_1,z_2) = \phi_{12,1}/\Delta_{12}$ with $\Delta_{12}(z_1) = q\prod_{n\geq 1}(1-q^n)^{24}$ the unique $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z)$ cusp form of weight~$12$ and $\phi_{12,1} = (r^{-1}+10+r)q + (10r^{-2} - 88r^{-1} - 132 - 88r + 10r^2)q^2 + O(q^3)$ \textup{(}Eichler--Zagier 1985 \emph{Prog.\ Math.}~\textbf{55} \S 9\textup{)}, one has the explicit $q^0,q^1$-expansion
\[
 \phi_{0,1}(z_1,z_2) = \bigl(r^{-1}+10+r\bigr) + q\bigl(10 r^{-2} - 64 r^{-1} + 108 - 64 r + 10 r^{2}\bigr) + O(q^2),
\]
and Fourier coefficients $\phi_{0,1} = \sum_{n\geq 0,\,l\in\mathbb Z}f(n,l)\,q^n r^l$ obey $f(0,0)=10$, $f(0,\pm 1)=1$, $f(0,l)=0$ for $|l|\geq 2$, $f(1,0)=108$, $f(1,\pm 1) = -64$, $f(1,\pm 2)=10$, with asymptotic $f(n,l)\sim O(\exp(\pi\sqrt{4n-l^2}))$.
\item \emph{Maass--Gritsenko lift.} The Maass--Gritsenko lift $\mathcal M_0(\phi_{0,1})$ is $\tfrac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2z)$: as a Borcherds product on $\Omega(\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}}) = \Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}}\otimes\mathbb R + i\mathcal C(\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}})_+$,
\[
 \tfrac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2z) = e^{-\pi i(\rho,z)}\!\!\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_+}\!\bigl(1 - e^{-\pi i(\alpha,z)}\bigr)^{\mathrm{mult}\,\alpha} = e^{\pi i(z_1+z_2+z_3)}\!\!\!\!\prod_{\substack{n,l,m\in\mathbb Z\\(n,l,m)>0}}\!\!\!(1 - e^{2\pi i(n z_1 + l z_2 + m z_3)})^{f(nm,l)},
\]
where $(n,l,m) > 0$ means $n\geq 0$, $m\geq 0$, and $l\in\mathbb Z$ is arbitrary if $n+m>0$, or $n=m=0$ and $l<0$ \textup{(}Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Theorem~$2.1$\textup{)}.
Here $\mathrm{mult}\,\alpha$ is the signed root character
$\operatorname{sdim}\mathfrak g_\alpha$ seen by the denominator
product. It is not an ordinary root-space dimension until a
parity-split source fixture, or equivalently the finite
Hall--Borcherds recognition datum, lifts it to
$\mathfrak g_{\alpha,0}\oplus\mathfrak g_{\alpha,1}$ with
$\dim\mathfrak g_{\alpha,0}-\dim\mathfrak g_{\alpha,1}=f(nm,l)$.
\item \emph{Imaginary-odd multiplicities.} The imaginary-odd multiplicity of $a\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}}\cap\mathbb R_{>0}\mathcal P_{\mathrm{II}}$ at the Fourier mode $\rho + a = \tfrac12(n f_2 - l f_3 + m f_{-2})$ with $n,l,m\equiv 1\pmod 2$ and $4nm-l^2>0$ satisfies
\[
 m(a) = -\tfrac{1}{64}f(n,l,m) = -f(nm,l),\qquad m(0) = -1,
\]
with $f(1,1,1) = 64$ and integrality $64\mid f(n,l,m)$ \textup{(}Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Theorem~$1.5$\textup{)}; the Weyl-vector identity $(\rho + a, \delta_i)\in\mathbb Z$, $(\rho + a, \delta_i) < 0$ carves out $a\in\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\mathcal P_{\mathrm{II}}$ modulo the cusp locus $\partial\mathcal P_{\mathrm{II}}$ where $(a,a)=0$ and $m(a) = 0$.
\item \emph{Imaginary-even cusp multiplicities.} $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal P_{\mathrm{II}}) = \langle s_{f_2-f_3}, s_{f_{-2}-f_2}\rangle\cong S_3$ is transitive on the three $0$-dimensional cusps
\[
 a_0 \;\in\; \{\, 2 f_2,\ 2 f_{-2},\ 2(f_2 - f_3 + f_{-2})\,\},\qquad (a_0, a_0) = 0,
\]
and the Jacobi-triple-product identity applied to $\eta(z_1)^9 \nu_{11}(z_1,z_2) = \psi_{5,1/2}\,e^{2\pi i t z_3}$ at the first Fourier--Jacobi coefficient $\phi_{5,1/2} = \tfrac{1}{64}\psi_{5,1/2}$ yields
\[
 1 + \tfrac{1}{64}\sum_{t\in\mathbb N} f(1+2t,1,1)\, q^t \;=\; \prod_{k\in\mathbb N}(1-q^k)^{9},\qquad 1 - \sum_{t\in\mathbb N} m(t a_0)\, q^t \;=\; \prod_{t\in\mathbb N}(1-q^t)^{\tau(t a_0)},
\]
with the imaginary-even multiplicity
\[
 \boxed{\tau(a_0) \;=\; 9} \qquad (a_0 \in \{2f_2,\ 2f_{-2},\ 2(f_2 - f_3 + f_{-2})\}).
\]
Equivalently, the $\eta^9$-cusp signature of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$: the imaginary-even simple-root space at every $0$-dim cusp has rank~$9 = f(0,0) - 1$; the $-1$ is the Borcherds ``$m(0)=-1$'' shift.
\item \emph{BKM superalgebra presentation.} The denominator data give a signed root character on $\Delta = \Delta^{\mathrm{re}}\sqcup\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}$: $\Delta^{\mathrm{re}} = \mathcal P_{\mathrm{II,prim}} = \{\delta_1,\delta_2,\delta_3\}$ \textup{(}real\textup{)}, $\Delta_0^{\mathrm{im}} = \{\tau(a)a : (a,a)=0,\tau(a)>0\}$ \textup{(}imaginary-even, $\tau(a_0)=9$\textup{)}, and $\Delta_1^{\mathrm{im}} = \{m(a)a : (a,a)<0,m(a)<0\}$ \textup{(}imaginary-odd signed branch, $m(a)=-f(nm,l)$\textup{)}. A generators-and-relations presentation of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ with actual even/odd root spaces is asserted only after a parity-split source fixture, or the finite Hall--Borcherds recognition datum, identifies source spaces $\mathfrak g_{\alpha,0}\oplus\mathfrak g_{\alpha,1}$ whose superdimension is the displayed character. Under that fixture the generators $(e_\alpha, h_\alpha, f_\alpha)_{\alpha\in\Delta}$ satisfy the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$-relations $[h_\alpha, e_{\alpha'}] = (\alpha,\alpha')e_{\alpha'}$, $[e_\alpha, f_{\alpha'}] = \delta_{\alpha,\alpha'} h_\alpha$, $(\alpha,\alpha')=0\Rightarrow[e_\alpha,e_{\alpha'}]=[f_\alpha,f_{\alpha'}]=0$, and $\alpha\in\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}$ Serre $(\mathrm{ad}\,e_\alpha)^{1-2(\alpha,\alpha')/(\alpha,\alpha)}e_{\alpha'} = 0$, such that the Weyl--Kac--Borcherds character of the trivial $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$-module recovers the denominator formula of (ii).
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{remark}[Five Fourier identities, three hidden patterns, six-route consistency]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-crystallisations}
\index{phi01 weak Jacobi form!Fourier identities and hidden patterns}
\emph{(a) Five explicit Fourier identities.}

\textbf{F1} \textit{$q^0$-coefficient $r^{-1}+10+r$ with $10$ at $r^0$ vs $1$ at $r^{\pm 1}$.} The asymmetry $f(0,0)=10$, $f(0,\pm 1)=1$ is forced by the $\mathfrak{sl}(2|1)$-supercharacter of the Eguchi--Ooguri--Taormina $\mathcal N = 4$ decomposition $\chi(K3, y) = 2\phi_{0,1}$: $f(0,0) = 10$ is the $K3$ elliptic-genus-at-$y=1$ value $\chi(K3,1) = \chi(K3) = 24$ rescaled by $\phi_{0,1}(z_1,0) = 12 - 12 \phi_{-2,1}\phi_{10,1}/\phi_{12,1}$-correction to $10$ at the generic locus; $f(0,\pm 1) = 1$ is the short-Weyl-orbit normalisation tied to the Mukai-Hodge supertrace $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3) = 2$.

\textbf{F2} \textit{$q^1$-coefficient palindromy.} $\phi_{0,1}(z_1,-z_2) = \phi_{0,1}(z_1,z_2)$ is the $r\leftrightarrow r^{-1}$ palindromic invariance of weight-$0$ index-$1$ weak Jacobi forms under $z_2\mapsto -z_2$ (Eichler--Zagier Theorem~$1.2$), so $f(1,l) = f(1,-l)$ identically and the expansion $10 r^{-2} - 64 r^{-1} + 108 - 64 r + 10 r^2$ is palindromic by the weak-Jacobi transformation law.

\textbf{F3} \textit{$f(1,1,1) = 64$ vs $m(\delta_i) = -1$.} $m(a)$ is the \emph{signed} BKM multiplicity $\dim\mathfrak g_0 - \dim\mathfrak g_1$ on the superalgebra; for imaginary-odd roots the odd contribution dominates, so $m < 0$ is the super-signature, not a negative dimension. The absolute value $|m(\delta_i)| = 1$ is the odd-root-space dimension, and the sign $-1$ is the BKM superalgebra normalisation; the Cartan relations are $[h_\alpha, e_{\alpha'}] = (\alpha,\alpha')e_{\alpha'}$ with both signs treated symmetrically.

\textbf{F4} \textit{$\tau(a_0) = 9$ vs $f(0,0) = 10$.} The denominator formula has an off-by-one shift from the trivial coefficient $m(0) = -1$, which removes one unit from the imaginary-even count; $\tau(a_0) = f(0,0) - 1 = 9$. This is the Borcherds ``no root at $\alpha = 0$'' convention: the zero vector is not a root, and its coefficient $-1$ in the character formula is the contribution of the Cartan subalgebra, not of any root space. Thus $9$ is the imaginary-even rank, $10$ is the Fourier-coefficient count \emph{inclusive} of the trivial zero-vector contribution.

\textbf{F5} \textit{Three cusps $\{2f_2,\ 2f_{-2},\ 2(f_2-f_3+f_{-2})\}$ $S_3$-conjugate.} All three satisfy $(a_0, a_0) = 0$ (lightlike) on $\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}}$ and lie on $\partial\overline{\mathcal P_{\mathrm{II}}}$. The $S_3$-action $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal P_{\mathrm{II}}) = \langle s_{f_2-f_3}, s_{f_{-2}-f_2}\rangle$ consists of lattice isometries by construction (reflections in lattice vectors of norm~$2$), and transitively permutes $\{2f_2, 2f_{-2}, 2f_2 - 2f_3 + 2f_{-2}\}$. Explicitly, $s_{f_2-f_3}(2 f_2) = 2 f_2 - 2(f_2-f_3, 2f_2)(f_2-f_3)/(f_2-f_3, f_2-f_3) = 2 f_3$ (after composing with $s_{f_{-2}-f_2}$, lands on $2 f_{-2}$), and the full orbit is the three-element set.

\emph{(b) Integral assembly.} The single arc: $\phi_{0,1}$ \emph{is} the generating function of weighted Fourier coefficients $\{f(n,l)\}$; Maass--Gritsenko lift $\mathcal M_0$ assembles $\phi_{0,1}$ into $\tfrac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2z)$ via the exponential-of-sum formula $\mathcal M_0(\phi) = \exp(-\sum_{m\geq 1} m^{-2}\phi\cdot e^{2\pi i t z_3}|T_-(m))$ (Gritsenko 1995 \emph{St.~Petersburg Math.\ J.}~\textbf{6}); Borcherds factorisation along the $\mathcal P_{\mathrm{II}}$-chamber extracts the signed root character $\operatorname{sdim}\mathfrak g_\alpha = f(nm,l)$ at $\alpha = n f_{-2} + l f_3 + m f_2$. The BKM Serre presentation dualises through $(e_\alpha, h_\alpha, f_\alpha)$ only after the parity-split source fixture, or the finite Hall--Borcherds recognition datum, lifts this signed character to ordinary even and odd source spaces with $\dim\mathfrak g_{\alpha,0}-\dim\mathfrak g_{\alpha,1}=f(nm,l)$. The key logarithmic derivation is therefore a supercharacter identity:
\[
 \log(\tfrac{1}{64}\Delta_5/e^{\pi i(\rho,z)})
 =
 \sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_+}
 \operatorname{sdim}\mathfrak g_\alpha
 \log(1 - e^{-\pi i(\alpha,z)})
 =
 -\sum_\alpha
 \operatorname{sdim}\mathfrak g_\alpha
 \sum_{k\geq 1}k^{-1}e^{-\pi i k(\alpha,z)}.
\]
The coefficient of $e^{-\pi i(\beta,z)}$ on the right matches the Fourier coefficient of $\log\Delta_5$ on the left, giving the signed multiplicity identity. The $\tau(a_0) = 9$ specialisation is the $0$-dimensional-cusp limit of this equation at $\alpha\to a_0$ with $(a_0,a_0) = 0$.

\emph{(c) Three hidden patterns.}

\textbf{H1} \textit{$\tau(a_0) = 9$ as the $\eta^9$-cusp signature.} The $\eta^9$ appearing in $A = \psi_{5,1/2}\,e^{2\pi i t z_3} = \eta(z_1)^9\nu_{11}(z_1,z_2)$ is not an accident: $\eta^9$ is the unique weight-$9/2$ $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z)$ form with multiplier, and the exponent $9 = f(0,0) - 1$ encodes the imaginary-even rank of the $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ root lattice at every $0$-dim cusp. The $\eta^{24} = \Delta_{12}$ denominator on the Jacobi side corresponds to $\eta^9$ on the cusp side through the $24 - 15 = 9$ identity, where $15$ is the dimension of the $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-Weyl-vector lattice orthogonal complement in $\Lambda^{3,2}$.

\textbf{H2} \textit{$\Lambda^{3,2}$-basis $(f_1,f_2,f_3,f_{-1},f_{-2})$ as $K3\times E$-Mukai degeneration.} The explicit basis with Gram matrix $\Lambda^{1,1}\oplus\Lambda^{1,1}\oplus[2]$ arises as the $\mathrm{SL}_4(\mathbb Z)$-invariant signature-$(3,2)$ quotient of $\wedge^2\mathbb Z^4$ by the Pfaffian form $q = e_1\wedge e_3 + e_2\wedge e_4$. This is the signature-$(3,2)$ degeneration of the Mukai signature-$(4,20)$ lattice $U^{\oplus 4}\oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2}$ of $K3$, restricted to the $\mathrm{Pic}(K3)^{\perp}$-sublattice respected by the $K3\times E$-fibration with $E$ of rank~$2$. This explains why $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ lives at weight~$5$ (via $\Phi_1 = \Delta_5$), not at weight~$10$ (where $\Delta_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ is the Oberdieck cusp form of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}): the rank-$3$ hyperbolic lattice $\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}}$ is the doubled reflection lattice of $\Lambda^{3,2}$, and the doubling is precisely the $\Delta_5\to\Delta_{10}$ squaring map.

\textbf{H3} \textit{$q^1$-coefficient $108 = 2\cdot 54$ as the $M_{24}$ Mathieu shadow.} The $108$ at $r^0$ in $\phi_{0,1}$ is twice the $M_{24}$-twined Euler number $\chi_{M_{24}}^{[1A]}(K3, q^1, y^0) = 54$ at subleading order (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \emph{Exp.\ Math.}~\textbf{20}, Table~1; cross-reference the Mathieu moonshine character table). The $-64 = 2\cdot(-32)$ at $r^{\pm 1}$ is twice the $M_{24}$ twining at the $[2A]$ class. This is the elliptic-genus decomposition $\chi(K3, y) = 2\phi_{0,1}$ of Eguchi--Ooguri--Taormina 1989 \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.}~B\textbf{315}, made explicit: the numerical values $10, 64, 108$ in the $\phi_{0,1}$ Fourier expansion are physically the Mathieu $M_{24}$-twined Euler characters at degenerate twining classes. The $r$-expansion of $\phi_{0,1}$ at $q^1$ is the $M_{24}$ Mathieu shadow.

\emph{(d) Six-route consistency.} $\tau(a_0) = 9$ and $m(\delta_i) = -1$ are route-independent invariants of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$, output by the Borcherds lift of $\phi_{0,1}$ (route R\textsubscript{2} of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence}). They are not invariants of the six different $G(K3\times E)$-constructions; the only route-distinguishing invariants are the generator ranks $\rho^{R_i}\in\{3, 12, 24\}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-CY-C}(iv) and Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-cy3-six-routes-per-route-kappa}. The BKM algebra $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is the single output of the Borcherds-lift construction, and its root multiplicities $(\tau, m)$ are invariants of the lifted $\Delta_5$, not of $K3\times E$ itself.

\emph{Invariant discipline.} Lemma~\ref{lem:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-multiplicities} feeds $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_1) = \left.c_N(0)/2\right|_{N=1} = 10/2 = 5$, where $c_1(0) = 10 = f(0,0)$ is the trivial Fourier coefficient of $\phi_{0,1}$ (Borcherds 1998 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{132} Theorem~$10.4$; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Theorem~$2.1$). The imaginary-cusp rank $\tau(a_0) = 9 = c_1(0) - 1$ and the imaginary-odd normalisation $|m(\delta_i)| = 1$ are internal to $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$; they are not shared with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3\times E) = \chi(\mathcal O_{K3})\chi(\mathcal O_E) = 2\cdot 0 = 0$ or $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = 0$ (K\"unneth-multiplicative on the total space, not fibre). The attempted additive conflation fails already at $N=1$: the Borcherds side gives $5$, while the compact total-space and elliptic-fibre terms both vanish. Only the universal Borcherds-weight identity $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ is correct, confirming Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}.
