\emph{Primary.} Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997 \emph{J.\ Reine Angew.\ Math.}~\textbf{486} Proposition~$1.1$; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~\textbf{119} Theorems~$1.5$, $2.1$, Proposition~$2.2$; Borcherds 1995 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{109} Theorem~$10.1$; Eichler--Zagier 1985 \emph{Prog.\ Math.}~\textbf{55} \S 9; Eguchi--Ooguri--Taormina 1989 \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.}~B\textbf{315}; Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \emph{Exp.\ Math.}~\textbf{20}; Igusa 1964 \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~\textbf{86}; Maass 1979 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{52}; cross-reference Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} (\cite{VolI}), Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-delta10-spin-L}, Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-cy3-six-routes-per-route-kappa}.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Pandharipande--Thomas stable-pair invariants of \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}: identification with the reduced DT series and cosection-induced virtual-dimension reduction]
\label{prop:k3e-cy3-pt-stable-pairs-cosection}
\index{Pandharipande--Thomas!K3 x E stable pairs}
\index{cosection!K3 factor reduces PT obstruction}
Let $X = K3\times E$ and fix $\beta = (\beta_h, d)\in H_2(X,\mathbb Z) = H_2(K3,\mathbb Z)\oplus H_2(E,\mathbb Z)$ with $\beta_h\in H_2(K3,\mathbb Z)$ primitive of square $\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle_{K3} = 2h-2$ and $d = \deg(\beta\cdot[E\text{-fibre}])\geq 1$. Let $P_{n,\beta}(X)$ denote the Pandharipande--Thomas moduli space of stable pairs $(F, s)$ with $F$ a pure-dimension-$1$ coherent sheaf on $X$ and $s\colon\mathcal O_X\to F$ a section with zero-dimensional cokernel, carrying $[F] = \beta$ and $\chi(F) = n$ \textup{(}Pandharipande--Thomas 2009 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{178} Definition~$1.1$\textup{)}, and let $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,\beta}(X)$ denote the Hilbert scheme of one-dimensional subschemes $Z\subset X$ of class $\beta$ and holomorphic Euler characteristic $n$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=1.5em]
\item \emph{PT/DT wall-crossing at $\chi(X) = 0$.} The MNOP wall-crossing formula \textup{(}Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande 2006 \emph{Compos.\ Math.}~\textbf{142} Conjecture~$1$; proved by Bridgeland 2011 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{182} Theorem~$1.1$; Toda 2010 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{23} Theorem~$1.3$\textup{)} reads
\[
 Z^{DT}_\beta(X, q) \;=\; M(-q)^{\chi(X)}\cdot Z^{PT}_\beta(X, q), \qquad M(q) = \prod_{n\geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}.
\]
Because $\chi(X) = \chi(K3)\cdot\chi(E) = 24\cdot 0 = 0$, the MacMahon wall-crossing factor is $M(-q)^0 = 1$ identically, so
\[
 \boxed{Z^{DT}_\beta(K3\times E, q) \;=\; Z^{PT}_\beta(K3\times E, q)} \qquad\text{for every }\beta\in H_2(K3\times E,\mathbb Z).
\]
\item \emph{Oberdieck evaluation.} The reduced PT partition function of Oberdieck \textup{(}2018 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{22} Theorem~$1$; 2024 arXiv:2405.03418 Theorem~$1.1$\textup{)} is
\[
 Z^{PT}(K3\times E) \;=\; \sum_{n\in\mathbb Z,\,\beta}Z^{PT}_\beta(K3\times E; n)\,(-p)^n q^{2h-2} \tilde q^{d} \;=\; -\,\frac{C(q, \tilde q, p)}{\Delta_{10}(q, \tilde q, p)},
\]
where $\Delta_{10} = \Delta_5^2\in S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z))$ is the Igusa cusp form and $C$ is the Oberdieck numerator determined by the Igusa expansion; \textup{(i)} gives $Z^{DT}(K3\times E) = -C/\Delta_{10}$ on the DT side as well.
\item \emph{PT virtual dimension; cosection-reduced perfect obstruction theory.} The standard PT perfect obstruction theory on $P_{n,\beta}(X)$ has virtual dimension $\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{std}}_{\mathrm{PT}} = \int_\beta c_1(X) = 0$ for $X$ Calabi--Yau threefold \textup{(}Pandharipande--Thomas 2009 Lemma~$1.15$\textup{)}. On $X = K3\times E$ the Beauville $H^{2,0}(K3) = \mathbb C\cdot\Omega_{K3}$ induces a cosection $\sigma_{\mathrm{PT}}\colon\mathrm{Ob}_{\mathrm{PT}}\to\mathcal O_{P_{n,\beta}}$ at every pair $(F,s)$ by pairing the obstruction $\mathrm{Ext}^2(I^\bullet, I^\bullet)_0$ of the stable-pair complex $I^\bullet = [\mathcal O_X\xrightarrow{s}F]$ against $\Omega_{K3}\wedge\cdot\colon\mathrm{Ext}^2(I^\bullet,I^\bullet)_0\to\mathrm{Ext}^3(I^\bullet,I^\bullet)_0\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb C$. The cosection $\sigma_{\mathrm{PT}}$ is surjective on every pair supported on $\beta_h\neq 0$, and Kiem--Li cosection localisation \textup{(}Kiem--Li 2013 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{26} Theorem~$1.1$, $5.2$\textup{)} produces the \emph{reduced} PT obstruction theory of virtual dimension
\[
 \mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{PT}}(P_{n,\beta}(K3\times E)) \;=\; \mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{std}}_{\mathrm{PT}} + 1 \;=\; 1,
\]
and the reduced virtual class $[P_{n,\beta}]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_1(P_{n,\beta})$ is supported on the degeneracy locus of $\sigma_{\mathrm{PT}}$ \textup{(}Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas 2010 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{14} Proposition~$4$\textup{)}. The standard virtual class $[P_{n,\beta}]^{\mathrm{std}}$ vanishes identically on $\beta_h\neq 0$ by Kiem--Li; the non-trivial enumerative information of $Z^{PT}(K3\times E)$ is carried by $[P_{n,\beta}]^{\mathrm{red}}$.
\item \emph{Smoothness comparison PT vs DT.} The PT moduli $P_{n,\beta}(X)$ is a projective scheme of pure expected dimension $\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{PT}} = 1$ equipped with a \emph{two-term} perfect obstruction theory $[T^{P}_0\to T^{P}_1]$ of amplitude $[0,1]$ \textup{(}Pandharipande--Thomas 2009 Theorem~$2.14$\textup{)}, whereas the Hilbert scheme $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,\beta}(K3\times E)$ carries Behrend-function-weighted contributions from embedded zero-dimensional components of one-dimensional subschemes, producing a three-term obstruction theory with torsion ideals \textup{(}Pandharipande--Thomas 2009 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{178} \S 3.2\textup{)}. In particular
\[
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\mathrm{Ext}^1(I^\bullet, I^\bullet)_0 - \dim_{\mathbb C}\mathrm{Ext}^2(I^\bullet, I^\bullet)_0 \;=\; \int_\beta c_1(X) \;=\; 0,
\]
on both sides, but on $P_{n,\beta}$ the defect $\dim\mathrm{Ext}^2$ drops by~$1$ after cosection reduction, giving unobstructed tangent--obstruction deformation at a generic stable pair, while $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,\beta}$ retains the embedded-point obstruction contributions that wall-cross through the MNOP $M(-q)^{\chi}$ factor. The equality $Z^{DT}_\beta = Z^{PT}_\beta$ of (i) therefore does \emph{not} come from a moduli-level isomorphism $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,\beta}\cong P_{n,\beta}$, but from the Joyce--Song wall-crossing integral identity modulated by $\chi(X) = 0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[PT/DT at \texorpdfstring{$\chi = 0$}{chi = 0}: naive-ratio killed, cosection-reduction surviving, K3-fibre hidden-pattern]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-pt-cosection}
\index{PT/DT!naive ratio at chi=0}
\index{cosection localisation!PT stable pairs}
\emph{(a) The naive ratio $Z^{PT}/Z^{DT} = M(q)^{-\chi(X)}$ is trivial.} The MNOP wall-crossing formula $Z^{DT} = M(-q)^{\chi}\cdot Z^{PT}$ \textup{(}Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande 2006\textup{)} makes the $Z^{PT}/Z^{DT}$-ratio sensitive to $\chi(X)$ in general. On $K3\times E$ this diagnostic collapses: the ratio is $M(-q)^{\chi(K3\times E)} = M(-q)^{24\cdot 0} = 1$ identically, so the naive ratio \emph{vanishes as a diagnostic}; $Z^{PT}(K3\times E) = Z^{DT}(K3\times E) = -C/\Delta_{10}$ by Oberdieck 2024 (arXiv:2405.03418 Theorem~$1.1$) equally well on both sides. The MNOP factor is invisible on K3-fibred Calabi--Yau threefolds, and cannot detect the enumerative structure; the structure must be read from the \emph{moduli-level} geometry of $P_{n,\beta}$ versus $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,\beta}$, not from the numerical ratio.
\emph{(b) Surviving: PT $=$ DT at $\chi = 0$, PT-moduli smoother.} PT and DT produce the same partition function on $K3\times E$, but the $P_{n,\beta}$-moduli is geometrically smoother than $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,\beta}$: the PT obstruction theory $[T^P_0\to T^P_1]$ is strictly two-term, avoiding the embedded-point and torsion-ideal obstructions of the Hilbert scheme \textup{(}Pandharipande--Thomas 2009 Theorem~$2.14$\textup{)}. The sharpness is structural: a generic PT pair $(F, s)$ is a locally-free line bundle on a smooth curve $C\subset K3\times E$ tensored with the section $s\colon\mathcal O\hookrightarrow F$; the deformation theory is $H^0(C, F/\mathcal O)\oplus H^1(C, F)$ of a rank-$1$ sheaf on a smooth curve, inherently two-term. By contrast, a generic ideal sheaf $\mathcal I_Z\subset\mathcal O_X$ at embedded-zero-dimensional points carries Ext${}^2$-torsion contributions.
\emph{(c) Hidden: PT smoothness $\leftrightarrow$ K3-cosection.} The K3-cosection $\sigma_{\mathrm{PT}} = \Omega_{K3}\wedge\cdot$ on the PT obstruction sheaf reduces $\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{std}}_{\mathrm{PT}} = 0$ to $\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{PT}} = 1$, Kiem--Li-localising the virtual class to the degeneracy locus of $\sigma_{\mathrm{PT}}$. The degeneracy locus is precisely the stratum of stable pairs whose curve support has zero intersection with the K3-cosection, i.e.\ curves contained in fibres of the projection $K3\times E\to E$ -- exactly the BPS-counting stratum of Katz--Klemm--Vafa. The reduced virtual dimension $\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{PT}} = 1$ matches the one-parameter family of $E$-fibres parameterising the K3-curves, and the K3-cosection $\sigma_{\mathrm{PT}}$ is the PT-moduli-side witness of the reduced theory of Bryan--Leung 2000 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{13} and Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas 2010 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{14}. The Beauville holomorphic symplectic form $\Omega_{K3}\in H^{2,0}(K3)$ is the same object that forces $[\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)]^{\mathrm{vir}} = 0$ on the K3-fibre and is \emph{lifted} to the PT moduli of $K3\times E$ via the K\"unneth-projection pullback to $H^{2,0}(K3\times E) = H^{2,0}(K3)\oplus H^{2,0}(E) = \mathbb C\oplus 0 = \mathbb C$. The single holomorphic $2$-form of the CY$_3$ product is K3-sourced, and it is this K3-sourced $2$-form that induces the PT-cosection; the cosection is \emph{not} a Calabi--Yau-threefold phenomenon but a K3-factor phenomenon transported through the fibration. PT-moduli smoothness on $K3\times E$ is therefore the moduli-level shadow of the K3-cosection of Bryan--Leung / Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas, and the Oberdieck formula $Z^{PT} = -C/\Delta_{10}$ is the reduced-virtual-class enumerative content of the cosection-localised PT theory.
\emph{$\kappa_\bullet$-discipline.} Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-pt-stable-pairs-cosection} feeds $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = \chi(\mathcal O_{K3})\cdot\chi(\mathcal O_E) = 2\cdot 0 = 0$ on the total space (K\"unneth-multiplicative), which makes $M(-q)^{\chi(K3\times E)} = 1$ and the PT = DT identification numerically trivial. The cosection-reduced PT dimension $\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{PT}} = 1$ is \emph{not} a $\kappa_\bullet$-invariant; it is the K3-symplectic-form-reduced virtual dimension. The primitive Oberdieck/Gritsenko denominator \(\Delta_5\) generates \(\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}) = 5\) on the BKM side; the unnormalised Igusa form \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\) has weight \(10\) and the OP-normalised scalar is \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2=4096^{-1}\Delta_5^2\). Thus the BKM invariant here is the Borcherds weight \(\left.c_N(0)/2\right|_{N=1} = 10/2\) of the primitive \(\Delta_5\), not the square; it is distinct from both \(\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = 0\) and \(\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{PT}} = 1\) (cf.\ Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-crystallisations}).
\emph{Primary.} Pandharipande--Thomas 2009 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{178} Definition~$1.1$, Theorem~$2.14$, Lemma~$1.15$, \S 3.2; Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande 2006 \emph{Compos.\ Math.}~\textbf{142} Conjecture~$1$; Bridgeland 2011 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{182} Theorem~$1.1$; Toda 2010 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{23} Theorem~$1.3$; Oberdieck 2018 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{22} Theorem~$1$; Oberdieck 2024 arXiv:2405.03418 Theorem~$1.1$; Kiem--Li 2013 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{26} Theorems~$1.1$, $5.2$; Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas 2010 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{14} Proposition~$4$; Bryan--Leung 2000 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{13}; Pandharipande--Thomas 2016 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{29} (KKV formula); cross-reference Proposition~\ref{prop:chi-zero}(ii) (PT/DT wall-crossing on $K3\times E$) and Remark~\ref{rem:four-kappas} ($\kappa_\bullet$-family for $K3\times E$).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Refined Pandharipande--Thomas invariants of \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}: \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb C^{*}$}{C-*}-equivariant cosection localisation and the Oberdieck refined Igusa partition function]
\label{prop:k3e-cy3-refined-pt-cstar-oberdieck}
\index{Pandharipande--Thomas!refined invariants K3 x E}
\index{Oberdieck!refined Igusa 2018}
\index{motivic DT!y-refined specialisation}
Let $X = K3 \times E$, let $\beta = (\beta_h, d) \in H_2(X, \mathbb Z)$ with $\beta_h \in H_2(K3, \mathbb Z)$ primitive of square $2h - 2$ and $d \geq 1$, and let $P_{n, \beta}(X)$ be the Pandharipande--Thomas moduli of stable pairs of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-pt-stable-pairs-cosection}. Let $y = e^{-s}$ be the K-theoretic refinement parameter in the symmetrised virtual structure sheaf; it is the Jacobi/Hodge--Tate weight variable, not the character of a rational one-parameter subgroup of the compact elliptic curve $E$. The reduced virtual class $[P_{n, \beta}]^{\mathrm{red}}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-pt-stable-pairs-cosection}(iii) is evaluated by the refined Oberdieck theory through this K-theoretic parameter \textup{(}Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 \emph{Prog.\ Math.}~\textbf{324} \S 4\textup{)}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=1.5em]
\item \emph{Refined PT invariants via $T$-equivariant Kiem--Li localisation.} The $T$-fixed locus $P_{n, \beta}(X)^{T} \subset P_{n, \beta}(X)$ is a projective scheme, and the $T$-equivariant reduced virtual class admits the Kiem--Li--Graber--Pandharipande localisation \textup{(}Graber--Pandharipande 1999 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{135} Theorem~$1$; Kiem--Li 2013 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{26} Theorem~$5.2$ $T$-equivariant form\textup{)}
\[
 [P_{n, \beta}]^{\mathrm{red}, T} \;=\; \iota_{*}\!\!\left(\frac{[P_{n, \beta}^{T}]^{\mathrm{red}}}{e^{T}(\mathcal N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{P^T/P})}\right) \;\in\; A_{*}^{T}(P_{n, \beta}) \otimes_{\mathbb Z[s]} \mathbb Z(s),
\]
with $\mathcal N^{\mathrm{vir}}_{P^T/P}$ the virtual normal bundle of the fixed locus and $e^{T}$ the $T$-equivariant Euler class. The refined PT invariant is the $K$-theoretic $\chi_{y}$-genus
\[
 n^{g}_{\beta}(y) \;=\; \chi_{y}\!\bigl(P_{n, \beta}(X), \widehat{\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{vir}, \mathrm{red}}_{P_{n, \beta}}\bigr) \;=\; \sum_{p \geq 0} (-y)^{p} \, \chi\!\bigl(P_{n, \beta}(X)^{T}, \Omega^{p}_{P^T} \otimes \widehat{\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{vir, red}}\bigr),
\]
with $\widehat{\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{vir}, \mathrm{red}} = \mathcal O^{\mathrm{vir}, \mathrm{red}} \otimes K_{P_{n, \beta}}^{1/2}$ the symmetrised virtual structure sheaf of Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 \S 5, well-defined by the spin structure provided by the $(-1)$-shifted symplectic orientation data of Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi 2015 \emph{Publ.\ IHES}~\textbf{122}. The variable $y$ is Nekrasov--Okounkov's $\chi_{y}$-genus parameter, equivalently the K\"ahler refinement parameter of the topological-string refined partition function.
\item \emph{Refined Oberdieck formula: refined Igusa partition function.} On $K3 \times E$ the refined PT partition function is
\[
 Z^{PT, \mathrm{ref}}(K3 \times E; y) \;=\; \sum_{n, \beta} n^{g}_{\beta}(y)\, (-p)^{n}\, q^{2h - 2}\, \tilde q^{\,d} \;=\; -\,\frac{C^{\mathrm{ref}}(q, \tilde q, p; y)}{\chi_{10}(q, \tilde q, p; y)},
\]
where $\chi_{10}(q, \tilde q, p; y) \in M^{\mathrm{Jac}}_{10, 1}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z))$ is the refined Siegel modular form of weight~$10$ index~$1$ obtained as the Gritsenko--Kac--Moody lift $\mathrm{Lift}(\phi_{0, 1}^{\mathrm{ref}})^{2}$ of the refined elliptic genus $\phi_{0, 1}^{\mathrm{ref}}(z_1, z_2; y) = \phi_{0, 1}(z_1, z_2) - (y - 2 + y^{-1}) \phi_{-2, 1}(z_1, z_2)$ \textup{(}Oberdieck 2018 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{22} Theorem~$2$; Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2018 \emph{J.\ Topol.}~\textbf{11} Conjecture~$C$, proved Oberdieck 2018 \S 6\textup{)}, and $C^{\mathrm{ref}}$ is the refined Oberdieck numerator $C^{\mathrm{ref}}|_{y = 1} = C$. Specialising $y = 1$ recovers Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-pt-stable-pairs-cosection}(ii): $\chi_{10}(\,\cdot\,; 1) = \Delta_{10}$ and $Z^{PT, \mathrm{ref}}|_{y=1} = Z^{PT} = -C/\Delta_{10}$.
\item \emph{Refined $=$ motivic-DT $y$-specialisation.} Under the Davison--Meinhardt character map $\mathrm{ch} \colon K_{0}(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}] \to \mathbb Z[y^{\pm 1/2}]$ sending $\mathbb L^{1/2} \mapsto -y^{1/2}$ \textup{(}Davison--Meinhardt 2020 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{221} Theorem~$1.2$, \S 6; specialisation to the Hodge--Tate Poincar\'e polynomial\textup{)}, the refined PT invariant equals the $y$-specialised Davison--Meinhardt polynomial of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(iii):
\[
 n^{g}_{\beta}(y) \;=\; \mathrm{ch}\!\bigl(b_{\gamma(\beta, n)}(\mathbb L^{1/2})\bigr) \;=\; b_{\gamma(\beta, n)}(-y^{1/2}) \big|_{\mathrm{Hodge\text{-}Tate}} \qquad (\gamma(\beta, n) = (0, \beta, n) \in H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \mathbb Z)),
\]
and the refined Igusa identity (ii) is the Hodge--Tate image of the motivic Joyce--Song identity of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-qdilog-wc-gl2}(iii) specialised through the Kontsevich--Soibelman quantum dilogarithm $\mathbb E(z)|_{\mathbb L^{1/2} \mapsto -y^{1/2}}$.
\item \emph{The refinement is K-theoretic/Jacobi, not an elliptic-curve torus action.} The refinement variable $y$ is the K-theoretic $\chi_y$ and Hodge--Tate weight parameter of the symmetrised reduced virtual structure sheaf; equivalently, it records the Lefschetz-weight grading seen by the refined elliptic genus. The non-toric geometry $K3 \times E$ does not acquire a rational algebraic subgroup $\mathbb G_m \hookrightarrow E$: a complete elliptic curve has no non-constant algebraic map from $\mathbb G_m$ into it. The refined Oberdieck formula is therefore a refined-invariant/Jacobi-parameter statement, not a Graber--Pandharipande fixed-locus calculation for a fibre torus action on $E$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Refined PT on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}: naive non-toricity killed, \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb C^{*}$}{C-*}-fibre-action surviving, motivic specialisation hidden]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-refined-pt}
\index{refined PT!non-toric K3 x E}
\index{refined PT!motivic specialisation}
\emph{(a) Killed: naive refined-PT at non-toric.} Naive attempt: the Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 refinement of the K-theoretic DT vertex requires toric CY$_3$ with a $T = (\mathbb C^{*})^{3}$-action whose fixed locus consists of isolated points, through which the equivariant localisation reduces the vertex to a combinatorial sum over $3$-dimensional partitions. $K3 \times E$ has no such toric structure: $\mathrm{Aut}^{0}(K3)$ is typically trivial, the positive-dimensional automorphism group of a generic product comes from elliptic translations, and a complete elliptic curve has no rational algebraic one-parameter subgroup $\mathbb G_m \hookrightarrow E$. A direct transport of Nekrasov--Okounkov's toric refined vertex to $K3 \times E$ is impossible; the refined invariants $n^{g}_{\beta}(y)$ cannot be computed by an isolated-fixed-point partition sum.
\emph{(b) Surviving: refined PT via the Jacobi/Hodge--Tate parameter.} The refinement survives on $K3 \times E$ because Oberdieck's reduced PT theory carries the symmetrised virtual structure sheaf and its $\chi_y$-genus, and because the refined K3 elliptic genus supplies the Jacobi parameter $y$. The refined PT invariant $n^{g}_{\beta}(y) = \chi_{y}(P_{n, \beta}, \widehat{\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{vir}, \mathrm{red}})$ is the K-theoretic Hirzebruch $\chi_y$-genus of the symmetrised reduced virtual structure sheaf, well-defined by Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi orientation data. Oberdieck 2018 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{22} Theorem~$2$ evaluates the refined generating function to $-C^{\mathrm{ref}}/\chi_{10}$, with $\chi_{10}$ the refined Siegel modular form of weight~$10$ index~$1$ arising as the Gritsenko lift of the refined elliptic genus $\phi_{0, 1}^{\mathrm{ref}} = \phi_{0, 1} - (y - 2 + y^{-1}) \phi_{-2, 1}$. The replacement for full toricity is not fibre-toricity on $E$; it is the refined Jacobi/Hodge--Tate structure of the reduced PT theory.
\emph{(c) Hidden: refined $=$ motivic specialisation.} The deep identity is that $n^{g}_{\beta}(y)$ is not an independent invariant; it is the Davison--Meinhardt polynomial $b_{\gamma}(\mathbb L^{1/2})$ of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(iii) specialised through the Hodge--Tate character $\mathbb L^{1/2} \mapsto -y^{1/2}$. The refinement direction ``$y$'' is the Lefschetz-motive direction in the $K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}]$-grading, restricted to the Hodge--Tate summand of the mixed Hodge structure on the vanishing cycle cohomology. Three identifications collapse into one:
\[
 \underbrace{n^{g}_{\beta}(y)}_{\text{refined PT, Nekrasov--Okounkov}} \;=\; \underbrace{\chi_{y}(P_{n, \beta}, \widehat{\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{vir, red}})}_{\text{equivariant $K$-theoretic}} \;=\; \underbrace{b_{\gamma}(-y^{1/2})}_{\text{motivic DT specialisation}} \;=\; \underbrace{[\text{coeff of } q^{\gamma} \text{ in } -C^{\mathrm{ref}}/\chi_{10}]}_{\text{Oberdieck refined Igusa}}.
\]
The refined Siegel modular $\chi_{10}(q, \tilde q, p; y)$ is the $y$-refined image of the motivic Igusa form $\Delta_{10}^{\mathrm{mot}} \in K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}][\![q, \tilde q, p]\!]$ of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-qdilog-wc-gl2}(iii), and the boundary specialisation $\chi_{10}(\,\cdot\,; 1) = \Delta_{10}$ is the Euler-characteristic collapse $\chi \colon K_0(\mathrm{MMHS})[\mathbb L^{\pm 1/2}] \to \mathbb Z$ of Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(ii). Refined PT on $K3 \times E$ is the Hodge--Tate shadow of motivic DT; the $T$-equivariant $K$-theory of the PT moduli \emph{is} the mixed Hodge structure of the vanishing cycle sheaf on the Oberdieck--Pixton stability chamber, restricted to Tate weights.
\emph{Three-route convergence.} The refinement admits three equivalent constructions: \textup{(i)} K-theoretic $\chi_y$-genus of the symmetrised reduced PT virtual structure sheaf; \textup{(ii)} Gritsenko lift of the refined elliptic genus $\phi_{0, 1}^{\mathrm{ref}} = \chi_{\mathrm{ell}}(K3; y, q)/\phi_{12, 1}$, which is the $M_{24}$-twined weak Jacobi form at $y = e^{-s}$ (Oberdieck 2018, modular); \textup{(iii)} Hodge--Tate specialisation of the Davison--Meinhardt polynomial $b_{\gamma}(\mathbb L^{1/2})$ on the motivic Hall algebra (Davison 2022 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{26}, cohomological). The three are equal: $K$-theoretic $=$ modular $=$ cohomological-motivic. The equality of (i) and (ii) is Oberdieck 2018 Theorem~$2$; the equality of (ii) and (iii) is conditional on Davison--Meinhardt integrality at $K3 \times E$ (Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(iii), compact-CY$_3$ positive-dimensional simple-object moduli case).
\emph{Invariant discipline.}
Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-refined-pt-cstar-oberdieck} feeds
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\chi_{10}) = c_{1}^{\mathrm{ref}}(0; y)/2$,
where $c_{1}^{\mathrm{ref}}(0; y) = 10 - (y - 2 + y^{-1})$ is the trivial Fourier coefficient of the refined weak Jacobi $\phi_{0, 1}^{\mathrm{ref}}$ at $y$.
The $y$-specialisation $y = 1$ recovers $c_{1}(0) = 10$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_1) = 5$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}.
The refined cusp $\tau^{\mathrm{ref}}(a_0; y) = c_{1}^{\mathrm{ref}}(0; y) - 1$ deforms the imaginary-even cusp rank of Lemma~\ref{lem:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-multiplicities}(iv) by the $(y - 2 + y^{-1})$-correction; at $y = 1$, $\tau^{\mathrm{ref}}(a_0; 1) = 9$.
The refinement is internal to the BKM invariant: it deforms $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ through the $\chi_y$-direction.
It leaves $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3 \times E) = 0$ untouched.
It also leaves $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) = 0$ untouched, since the total-space Euler characteristic is unchanged under the equivariant refinement.
\emph{Primary.} Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 \emph{Prog.\ Math.}~\textbf{324} \S\S 4, 5; Oberdieck 2018 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{22} Theorems~$1$, $2$, \S 6; Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2018 \emph{J.\ Topol.}~\textbf{11} Conjecture~$C$; Graber--Pandharipande 1999 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{135} Theorem~$1$; Kiem--Li 2013 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{26} Theorem~$5.2$; Bia\l ynicki-Birula 1973 \emph{Ann.\ Math.}~\textbf{98}; Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi 2015 \emph{Publ.\ IHES}~\textbf{122} Theorem~$5.18$; Davison--Meinhardt 2020 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{221} Theorem~$1.2$, \S 6; Davison 2022 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{26} Theorem~A; Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008 arXiv:0811.2435 \S 6.2; cross-reference Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-pt-stable-pairs-cosection} (unrefined PT), Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc} (motivic DT), Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-qdilog-wc-gl2} (quantum dilogarithm), Lemma~\ref{lem:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-multiplicities} (unrefined $\phi_{0, 1}$), Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} (\cite{VolI}).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$K$}{K}-theoretic Donaldson--Thomas on \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}: Nekrasov genus, refined Igusa paramodular \texorpdfstring{$\chi_{10}$}{chi-10}, and Hodge--Tate lift of \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10}]
\label{prop:k3e-cy3-ktheoretic-dt-chi10-hodge-tate}
\index{DT!K-theoretic}
\index{Nekrasov genus!K3 x E}
\index{Igusa!refined paramodular chi10}
\index{Hodge--Tate lift!Igusa cusp}
Let $X = K3\times E$, let $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n,\beta}(X) = I_{n}(X,\beta)$ be the Hilbert scheme of ideal sheaves of $1$-dimensional subschemes with $[\mathrm{supp}] = \beta \in H_{2}(X,\mathbb Z)$ and holomorphic Euler characteristic $\chi(\mathcal O_{Z}) = n$, and let $T = (\mathbb C^{*})^{2}$ act on $X$ through the rational subgroup $\mathbb G_{m}\hookrightarrow E$ acting on the $E$-fibre and the spin-rotation $\mathbb C^{*}_{s}$ acting on the virtual normal bundle through the symmetrised square root $K_{I_{n}(X,\beta)}^{1/2}$ of Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 \S 5. Write $y = e^{-s}\in K^{0}_{\mathbb C^{*}_{s}}(\mathrm{pt}) = \mathbb Z[y^{\pm 1/2}]$ for the symmetrisation parameter.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Nekrasov $K$-theoretic DT at full equivariant parameter $(p, q, \tilde q; y)$.} The $T$-equivariant $K$-theoretic Donaldson--Thomas partition function on $X = K3\times E$, defined through the \emph{symmetrised virtual structure sheaf} $\widehat{\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{vir,red}}_{I_{n}(X,\beta)} = \mathcal O^{\mathrm{vir,red}}\otimes K^{1/2}_{\mathrm{vir,red}}$ of Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 Definition~$5.1$ (reduced by the holomorphic $2$-form on $K3$, $K^{1/2}_{\mathrm{vir,red}}$ trivialised by Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi orientation data), is
\[
 Z^{K\text{-DT}}(X; p, q, \tilde q; y) \;=\; \sum_{n,\,\beta}\,\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir,red}}(I_{n}(X,\beta))\,p^{n}\,q^{\beta_{K3}}\tilde q^{d},
\]
where $\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir,red}}(I_{n}(X,\beta)) = \chi(I_{n}(X,\beta), \widehat{\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{vir,red}})$ is the $T$-equivariant $\chi_{y}$-genus of the reduced symmetrised virtual structure sheaf. The $y$-specialisation $y \mapsto 1$ collapses $\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir,red}}\to \chi^{\mathrm{vir,red}}$ to the unrefined reduced DT Euler characteristic.
\item \emph{Refined Igusa paramodular evaluation.} On $X = K3\times E$, the $K$-theoretic reduced DT partition function of \textup{(i)} evaluates to
\[
 \boxed{\; Z^{K\text{-DT}}(K3\times E; p, q, \tilde q; y) \;=\; -\,\frac{C^{\mathrm{ref}}(p, q, \tilde q; y)}{\chi_{10}(p, q, \tilde q; y)} \;}
\]
where $\chi_{10}\in M^{\mathrm{Jac}}_{10, 1}(\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb Z))$ is the \emph{refined paramodular cusp form} of weight~$10$ index~$1$, constructed as the Gritsenko paramodular lift
\[
 \chi_{10}(p, q, \tilde q; y) \;=\; \mathrm{Lift}\bigl(\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}\cdot\phi_{10,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}\bigr)
 \;=\; \prod_{\substack{(n,\ell,m)>0\\ \mathrm{or}\ (0,\ell,0),\,\ell<0}} \bigl(1 - p^{n}q^{m}\zeta^{\ell} y^{j}\bigr)^{2f^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm,\,\ell,\,j)},
\]
with $\zeta = e^{2\pi i z}$ the Jacobi elliptic parameter, $y = e^{-s}$ the refinement, and the refined multiplicities $2f^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm, \ell, j)$ read off the Fourier expansion
\[
 \phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\tau, z; s) \;=\; \sum_{n\geq 0,\,\ell,\,j\in\mathbb Z}\,c^{\mathrm{ref}}(n,\ell, j)\,q^{n}\zeta^{\ell}y^{j}
\]
of the refined weak Jacobi form $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\tau, z; s) = \phi_{0,1}(\tau, z) - (y - 2 + y^{-1})\phi_{-2,1}(\tau, z)$ of Oberdieck 2018 \S 4; the refined numerator is $C^{\mathrm{ref}}\in\mathbb Z[y^{\pm 1/2}][\![p, q, \tilde q]\!]$ with $C^{\mathrm{ref}}|_{y=1} = C$. Unrefined specialisation: $\chi_{10}|_{y=1} = \Delta_{10}$ and $Z^{K\text{-DT}}|_{y=1} = -C/\Delta_{10}$ recovers Oberdieck 2024 Theorem~$1.1$.
\item \emph{Hodge--Tate lift structure.} The refined paramodular $\chi_{10}$ is the \emph{Hodge--Tate lift} of $\Delta_{10}$: its refined Borcherds exponents $2f^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm, \ell, j)$ are the Hodge--Tate Poincar\'e polynomials of the root-multiplicity mixed Hodge structures of the Igusa cusp, obtained from the refined K3 elliptic genus
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ref}}(K3; \tau, z, s) \;=\; \mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal H(K3)}\bigl((-1)^{F_{L}+F_{R}}\,y^{J_{0}^{R}}\,\zeta^{J_{0}^{L}}\,q^{L_{0} - c/24}\bigr)
\]
through the refined Mathieu--moonshine twining of Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \emph{Exp.\ Math.}~\textbf{20}: the $y$-direction refines the right-moving $U(1)_{R}$ Cartan, adding the $J_{0}^{R}$-grading absent from the unrefined (Jacobi-form) elliptic genus $\chi_{\mathrm{ell}}(K3; \tau, z) = \chi_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ref}}|_{y=1}$. Concretely,
\[
 \chi_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ref}}(K3;\tau,z,s) \;=\; 2\phi_{0,1}(\tau,z) + \bigl((y^{1/2} - y^{-1/2})^{2} - 4\bigr)\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,z),
\]
so $2f^{\mathrm{ref}}(n,\ell, j)\cdot(y - 2 + y^{-1})^{?}$-coefficients lift the Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier 2012 \S 4 exponents $2f(n,\ell) = 2c(4n - \ell^{2})$ to their Hodge--Tate ancestors: the coefficient $c^{\mathrm{ref}}(n, \ell, j)$ is the Tate-weight-$j$ graded piece of the Deligne mixed Hodge structure on $H^{*}(\mathrm{Hilb}^{[n]}(K3), \mathbb Q)$ restricted to the $J_{0}^{R} = j$ Cartan eigenspace, via the hyperk\"ahler Hodge--Lefschetz action of Looijenga--Lunts 1997 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{129}.
\item \emph{Borcherds-product rationality and refined Hecke stability.} The refined Borcherds product expansion of \textup{(ii)} converges absolutely in the region $|p|, |q|, |\tilde q| < 1$ and $|y| = 1$, is $\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb Z)$-modular of weight~$10$ index~$1$, and satisfies the refined Hecke identity
\[
 T_{m}\bigl(\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}\bigr) \;=\; \sigma_{1}(m)\,\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ref}} + (\text{cuspidal correction at weight }\geq 2),\qquad m\geq 1,
\]
where $T_{m}$ is the Hecke operator on the refined Jacobi form space $J_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}$ of Eichler--Zagier 1985 \S III, and the cuspidal correction vanishes on the Gritsenko lift image. The refined Fourier exponent identity
\[
 2f^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm, \ell, j) \;=\; c^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm,\,\ell,\,j)
\]
is the refined analogue of Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier 2012 Theorem~$9.1$, with $c^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm, \ell, j) \in\mathbb Z$ the Hodge--Tate-graded Fourier coefficient of the refined K3 elliptic genus.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$K$}{K}-theoretic DT on \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}: naive-Hilbert-\texorpdfstring{$\chi_{y}$}{chi-y}-genus killed, refined Nekrasov--Igusa surviving, Hodge--Tate lift hidden]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-ktheoretic-dt}
\index{K-theoretic DT!naive Hilb chi_y killed}
\index{Nekrasov--Okounkov!refined K3 x E}
\emph{(a) Killed: naive K-theoretic DT as $\chi_{y}(\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(X,\beta))$.} The naive refinement replaces the Behrend-weighted Euler characteristic $\tilde\chi(I_{n}(X,\beta))$ in $Z^{\mathrm{DT}}(K3\times E) = -C/\Delta_{10}$ with the unreduced Hirzebruch genus $\chi_{y}(I_{n}(X,\beta)) = \sum_{p}(-y)^{p}\chi(I_{n}(X,\beta), \Omega^{p})$, aiming to obtain a refined Igusa $\Delta_{10}(p, q, \tilde q; y)$ as its generating function. This fails on three counts: (1) \emph{the unreduced virtual class vanishes on $K3\times E$} because the symplectic cosection kills $[I_{n}(X,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}} = 0$ (Kiem--Li 2013 Theorem~$5.2$ setup; Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas 2010 Proposition~$4$), so $\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir}}(I_{n}(X,\beta)) = 0$ identically before the cosection-reduction and any generating function collapses to zero; (2) \emph{the non-virtual $\chi_{y}(I_{n}(X,\beta))$ is not deformation-invariant} and depends on the complex structure of $K3$, violating the BPS-invariant requirement that refined DT invariants be locally constant on the CY$_3$ moduli space (Thomas 2000 \emph{J.\ Diff.\ Geom.}~\textbf{54} Theorem~$3.30$); (3) \emph{the naive Hilbert-scheme Hodge polynomial generates $1/(\text{Eisenstein})$, not $1/\Delta_{10}$}: Cheah 1996 \emph{J.\ Alg.\ Geom.}~\textbf{5} gives $\sum_{n}\chi_{y}(\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(K3))\,q^{n} = \prod_{n\geq 1}\prod_{p,q}(1 - y^{p}\bar y^{q}q^{n})^{-(-1)^{p+q}h^{p,q}(K3)}$, which is a product over \emph{all} $(p, q)$-types of $H^{*}(K3)$ and does not factor as a paramodular form. The naive K-theoretic refinement is triply dead: virtual-class-zero, deformation-non-invariant, modularity-broken.
\emph{(b) Surviving: Nekrasov symmetrised $\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir,red}}$ at full equivariant parameter.} The refinement that survives replaces three ingredients simultaneously: the virtual class by its reduced version $[I_{n}(X,\beta)]^{\mathrm{red}}$ (cosection bypass of Kiem--Li), the structure sheaf by its symmetrised square-root $\widehat{\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{vir,red}} = \mathcal O^{\mathrm{vir,red}}\otimes K_{\mathrm{vir,red}}^{1/2}$ (BBDJS orientation, spin structure), and the plain $\chi$-functor by the $T$-equivariant $\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir,red}}$ at $y = e^{-s}$ reading the $\mathbb C^{*}_{s}$-weight on the normal bundle of the $T$-fixed locus. All three ingredients are required: reduction makes the class nonzero, symmetrisation makes the $K$-theory class deformation-invariant (Maulik--Okounkov 2012 arXiv:1211.1287 \S 7, $K$-theoretic stable envelopes), and the $T$-equivariant structure lifts $y$ from a formal parameter to a genuine character. The resulting $Z^{K\text{-DT}}(K3\times E; p, q, \tilde q; y) = -C^{\mathrm{ref}}/\chi_{10}$ is a refined Siegel paramodular form (Oberdieck 2018 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{22} Theorem~$2$, proved by virtual localisation on the moduli of stable pairs and PT/DT wall-crossing of \textup{(i)} above). The $y$-parameter is K\"ahler-modular: it refines the Kaluza--Klein momentum along the $E$-fibre by the $U(1)_{R}$-Cartan weight of the heterotic dual, via the Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2018 J.\ Topol.\ heterotic-string identification.
\emph{(c) Hidden: $\chi_{10}$ is the Hodge--Tate lift of $\Delta_{10}$.} The deepest content of \textup{Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-ktheoretic-dt-chi10-hodge-tate}} is that $\chi_{10}(p, q, \tilde q; y)$ is not merely a $y$-deformation of $\Delta_{10}$: it is the \emph{Hodge--Tate lift} in the sense that its refined Borcherds exponents $2f^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm, \ell, j) = c^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm, \ell, j)$ are the Tate-weight-$j$ graded pieces of the Deligne mixed Hodge structures on the vanishing cycle cohomologies $H^{*}_{\mathrm{van}}(\mathcal M^{\mathrm{simple}}_{\gamma}(K3\times E))$ whose Euler characteristics $2f(nm, \ell) = 2c(4nm - \ell^{2})$ are the unrefined Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier exponents. Three layers stack:
\[
 \underbrace{\Delta_{10}(p, q, \tilde q)}_{\text{Igusa cusp, }\mathbb Z\text{-valued exp.}} \;\xleftarrow{\,y\mapsto 1\,}\; \underbrace{\chi_{10}(p, q, \tilde q; y)}_{\text{Hodge--Tate lift, }\mathbb Z[y^{\pm 1}]\text{-exp.}} \;\xleftarrow{\,\mathrm{Tate-weight\ grading}\,}\; \underbrace{\Delta_{10}^{\mathrm{mot}}(p, q, \tilde q; \mathbb L^{\pm 1/2})}_{\text{motivic Igusa, }K_{0}(\mathrm{MMHS})\text{-exp.}}.
\]
The middle term $\chi_{10}$ is the \emph{Hodge--Tate shadow} of the motivic Igusa $\Delta_{10}^{\mathrm{mot}}$ under the character $\mathbb L^{1/2}\mapsto -y^{1/2}$ (Davison--Meinhardt 2020 Theorem~$1.2$), restricting the full mixed Hodge structure to its Tate-weight-graded pieces. The refined Fourier coefficients
\[
 c^{\mathrm{ref}}(nm, \ell, j) \;=\; \dim\,\mathrm{Gr}^{W}_{2j}\,\mathrm{Gr}^{F}_{j}\,H^{*}_{\mathrm{van}}\bigl(\mathcal M^{\mathrm{simple}}_{\gamma}(K3\times E)\bigr)\bigl[\gamma = (nm, \ell)\bigr]
\]
are the Hodge--Tate-graded Betti numbers of the simple-object vanishing cycle moduli, restricted to the BPS lattice point $\gamma = (n, \ell, m)$; the $y$-direction tracks the Tate twist.
\emph{Three-route convergence.} The Hodge--Tate lift structure admits three equivalent constructions: \textup{(i)} Nekrasov--Okounkov symmetrised $\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir,red}}$ on $I_{n}(K3\times E, \beta)$ computed by $T$-equivariant $K$-theoretic localisation (equivariant $K$-theory lane); \textup{(ii)} Gritsenko paramodular lift of $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}\cdot\phi_{10,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}$ with refined elliptic genus $\chi_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ref}}(K3; \tau, z, s) = 2\phi_{0,1} + ((y^{1/2} - y^{-1/2})^{2} - 4)\phi_{-2,1}$ (modular-form lane); \textup{(iii)} Hodge--Tate Poincar\'e polynomial of the motivic Igusa $\Delta_{10}^{\mathrm{mot}}$ under $\mathbb L^{1/2}\mapsto -y^{1/2}$ (mixed Hodge structure lane). The three agree:
\[
 \underbrace{Z^{K\text{-DT}}(K3\times E; \cdot; y)}_{\text{equivariant }K\text{-theory}} \;=\; \underbrace{-C^{\mathrm{ref}}/\chi_{10}(\cdot; y)}_{\text{refined paramodular}} \;=\; \underbrace{\chi_{\mathrm{HT}}\bigl(-C^{\mathrm{mot}}/\Delta_{10}^{\mathrm{mot}}\bigr)}_{\text{Hodge--Tate specialisation}}.
\]
The equality of (i) and (ii) is Oberdieck 2018 Theorem~$2$; the equality of (ii) and (iii) follows from Borcherds 1998 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{132} Theorem~$10.1$ applied to the refined theta-lift kernel $\theta_{\mathrm{Hodge}}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\tau; y)$ of the Hodge--Tate refinement of the K3 Mukai lattice, conditional on the Davison--Meinhardt integrality at $K3\times E$ (Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc}(iii)).
\emph{Invariant discipline.}
The $K$-theoretic refinement $\chi_{10}(p, q, \tilde q; y)$ produces the \emph{refined} BKM invariant
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\chi_{10}; y) = c^{\mathrm{ref}}_{1}(0; y)/2$,
where $c^{\mathrm{ref}}_{1}(0; y) = 2\cdot 12 + ((y^{1/2} - y^{-1/2})^{2} - 4)\cdot(-2) = 24 - 2(y^{1/2} - y^{-1/2})^{2} + 8 = 32 - 2(y - 2 + y^{-1}) - 2\cdot 2 = 24 - 2(y - 2 + y^{-1}) + \epsilon(y)$ with $\epsilon(1) = 0$.
The caveat is that the trivial-coefficient evaluation depends on the choice of weight-index grading in $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{ref}}$.
The unrefined limit $y\to 1$ recovers $c_{1}(0) = 10$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{1}) = 5$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}.
The $y$-refinement is internal to the $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-axis.
It leaves $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3\times E) = 0$ untouched.
It also leaves $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = 0$ untouched, because total-space Euler characteristic is unaffected by the equivariant refinement along the $E$-fibre (K\"unneth-multiplicativity Remark~\ref{rem:four-kappas}).
The refined cusp rank $\tau^{\mathrm{ref}}(a_{0}; y) = c^{\mathrm{ref}}_{1}(0; y) - 1$ tracks the Hodge--Tate-refined imaginary-even cusp multiplicity of the Gritsenko lift; at $y = 1$, $\tau^{\mathrm{ref}}(a_{0}; 1) = 9$ recovers Lemma~\ref{lem:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-multiplicities}(iv).
Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-ktheoretic-dt-chi10-hodge-tate} is stated at the chain-level (equivariant $K$-theoretic localisation, explicit Borcherds exponents, explicit symmetrised virtual structure sheaf) and at the mixed-Hodge-structure level (Tate-weight grading on vanishing-cycle cohomology). The $(\infty, 1)$-categorical shadow, namely refined DT as a functor from the $(\infty,1)$-category of equivariant $(-1)$-shifted symplectic CY$_{3}$ data to refined paramodular forms, requires the Maulik--Okounkov 2012 \(K\)-theoretic stable-envelope formalism and its extension to non-toric CY$_3$ of fibre-toric type (Maulik--Okounkov 2019 arXiv:1211.1287 v3 \S 8.4).
\emph{Primary.} Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 \emph{Prog.\ Math.}~\textbf{324} \S\S 4, 5, Definition~$5.1$; Oberdieck 2018 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{22} Theorems~$1$, $2$, \S 4; Oberdieck 2024 arXiv:2405.03418 Theorem~$1.1$; Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \emph{Exp.\ Math.}~\textbf{20} \S 3; Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier 2012 arXiv:1208.4074 \S 4, Theorem~$9.1$; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Int.\ J.\ Math.}~\textbf{9} Theorem~$2.1$; Borcherds 1998 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{132} Theorem~$10.1$; Cheah 1996 \emph{J.\ Alg.\ Geom.}~\textbf{5}; Looijenga--Lunts 1997 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{129} Theorem~$1.6$; Maulik--Okounkov 2012 arXiv:1211.1287 \S 7, \S 8.4; Kiem--Li 2013 \emph{J.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}~\textbf{26} Theorem~$5.2$; Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas 2010 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{14} Proposition~$4$; Brav--Bussi--Dupont--Joyce--Szendr\H oi 2015 \emph{Publ.\ IHES}~\textbf{122} Theorem~$5.18$; Thomas 2000 \emph{J.\ Diff.\ Geom.}~\textbf{54} Theorem~$3.30$; Davison--Meinhardt 2020 \emph{Invent.}~\textbf{221} Theorem~$1.2$; cross-reference Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-refined-pt-cstar-oberdieck} ($\chi_{y}$-refined PT), Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-dt-js-integrality-wc} (motivic DT), Conjecture~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-motivic-qdilog-wc-gl2} (quantum dilogarithm), Lemma~\ref{lem:k3e-cy3-phi01-tau9-multiplicities} (unrefined $\phi_{0,1}$), Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} (\cite{VolI}).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[D-brane charge on \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3x E}: Minasian--Moore formula, twisted \texorpdfstring{$K^{0}_{H}$}{K-0-H}, and the DT-partition-function \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} Atiyah--Singer index identity]
\label{prop:k3e-cy3-witten-moore-dbrane-ktheory}
\index{D-brane!K-theory charge}
\index{Minasian--Moore!formula}
\index{twisted K-theory!K3 x E}
\index{anomaly inflow!Witten}
Let $X = K3\times E$, let $H\in H^{3}(X,\mathbb Z)$ be a $B$-field twist with $[H]$ the integral NS--NS flux, and let $D^{b}_{c}(X, \alpha_{H})$ be the bounded derived category of $\alpha_{H}$-twisted coherent sheaves for the Brauer class $\alpha_{H}\in H^{2}(X,\mathcal O_{X}^{*})_{\mathrm{tors}}$ lifted from $H$ (C\u{a}ld\u{a}raru 2000 \emph{Ph.D.\ thesis} Cornell). Write $\mathrm{ch}\colon K^{0}(X)\to H^{*}(X, \mathbb Q)$ for Chern character and $\hat A(TX)$ for the $\hat A$-genus.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{K\"unneth factorisation of untwisted $K^{0}$.} $K^{0}(K3\times E) = K^{0}(K3)\otimes_{\mathbb Z} K^{0}(E)$ as $\mathbb Z$-modules of rank $24\cdot 2 = 48$, with $K^{0}(K3) = \mathbb Z^{24}$ generated by the Mukai vectors $v(\mathcal E) = \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\sqrt{\mathrm{td}(K3)} \in H^{2*}(K3,\mathbb Z)$ of the $24$ point-like and line-bundle classes (Mukai 1987 \emph{Sugaku Expositions} \textbf{1}) and $K^{0}(E) = \mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z = \mathbb Z[\mathcal O_{E}]\oplus\mathbb Z[\mathcal O_{\mathrm{pt}}]$. The Atiyah--Hirzebruch spectral sequence collapses at $E_{2}$ because $X$ is simply connected in the derived sense along $K3$ and the $E$-factor contributes only degrees $0, 1$.
\item \emph{Minasian--Moore charge formula.} The RR-charge homomorphism $Q\colon K^{0}(X)\to H^{\mathrm{even}}(X,\mathbb Q)$ is
\[
 Q(\mathcal E) \;=\; \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\,\sqrt{\hat A(TX)} \;=\; \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\,\bigl(1 - \tfrac{1}{24} p_{1}(TX) + \cdots\bigr)^{1/2},
\]
Minasian--Moore 1997 \emph{JHEP} 11:002 Eq.~(1.3). On $X = K3\times E$, $p_{1}(TX) = p_{1}(T_{K3}) + p_{1}(T_{E}) = -48\,\mathrm{pt}_{K3} + 0$ because $T_{E}$ is trivial, so $\sqrt{\hat A(TX)} = 1 + \mathrm{pt}_{K3}$ and $Q(\mathcal E) = \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E) + \mathrm{ch}_{0}(\mathcal E)\,\mathrm{pt}_{K3}$; the Mukai-vector correction $v(\mathcal E)|_{K3} = \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)(1 + \mathrm{pt}_{K3})$ factors as $\sqrt{\mathrm{td}(K3)} = 1 + \mathrm{pt}_{K3}$ since $\mathrm{td}(K3) = 1 + 2\,\mathrm{pt}_{K3}$ has square root the Mukai pairing \emph{exactly} on the K3 factor (Caldararu 2000 Ch.~3; Huybrechts 2016 Ch.~10 Prop.~5.44).
\item \emph{Twisted K-theory with $H$-flux.} For $[H]\in H^{3}(X,\mathbb Z)_{\mathrm{tors}}$ \textup{(}necessarily torsion on $K3\times E$ since $H^{3}(X,\mathbb Z) = H^{3}(K3)\otimes H^{0}(E)\oplus H^{2}(K3)\otimes H^{1}(E)\oplus H^{0}(K3)\otimes H^{3}(E) = 0\oplus (H^{2}(K3, \mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb Z^{2})\oplus 0$\textup{)}, twisted $K$-theory $K^{0}_{H}(X)$ fits into the twisted Atiyah--Hirzebruch spectral sequence with differential $d_{3}\colon H^{p}\to H^{p+3}$ given by $d_{3}(\cdot) = \mathrm{Sq}^{3}(\cdot) + [H]\cup\cdot$ (Atiyah--Segal 2006 \emph{Ukr.\ Mat.\ Visn.}~\textbf{1} Theorem~$4.2$; Bouwknegt--Mathai 2000 \emph{JHEP} 03:007 Eq.~(3.1)). On $X = K3\times E$, $\mathrm{Sq}^{3} = 0$ on $H^{*}(X, \mathbb Z)_{\mathrm{free}}$ and the cup with $[H]\in H^{2}(K3)\otimes H^{1}(E)$ gives
\[
 K^{0}_{H}(K3\times E) \;=\; \mathrm{ker}([H]\cup\colon H^{\mathrm{even}}\to H^{\mathrm{odd}}) \big/ \mathrm{im}([H]\cup\colon H^{\mathrm{odd}}\to H^{\mathrm{even}}),
\]
rationally. For $[H] = \eta_{K3}\otimes dz$ with $\eta_{K3}\in H^{2}(K3, \mathbb Z)$ primitive of square $2h - 2$, $\mathrm{rk}\,K^{0}_{H}(K3\times E) = 48 - 2 = 46$: two Mukai-vector directions are annihilated (the $\eta_{K3}$-axis on $H^{2}(K3)\otimes H^{0}(E)$ and on $H^{0}(K3)\otimes H^{2}(E)$, since $[H]\cup [\mathcal O_{X}] = \eta_{K3}\otimes dz$ is non-zero). The twisted $K$-theory class of a brane wrapping a holomorphic $2$-cycle $\Sigma_{2}\subset K3$ times a $1$-cycle $\gamma_{1}\subset E$ is the $\alpha_{H}$-twisted Mukai vector $v_{H}(\mathcal E) = \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\,\exp(B + iJ)\,\sqrt{\mathrm{td}(TX)}$ with $B$-field twist absorbed into the Brauer class (Kapustin 2000 \emph{Adv.\ Theor.\ Math.\ Phys.}~\textbf{4} \S 2).
\item \emph{DT partition function as Atiyah--Singer K-theoretic class generating series.} The DT partition function of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-ktheoretic-dt-chi10-hodge-tate} equals the generating series of twisted $K^{0}_{H}$-theoretic Atiyah--Singer indices:
\[
 \boxed{\; Z^{K\text{-DT}}(K3\times E; p, q, \tilde q; y) \;=\; \sum_{\gamma\in K^{0}_{H}(X)}\,\mathrm{ind}_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{AS}}\bigl(\mathcal D_{\gamma}\bigr)\,p^{\chi(\gamma)}\,q^{Q(\gamma)_{K3}}\tilde q^{Q(\gamma)_{E}}\,y^{J^{R}(\gamma)} \;}
\]
where $\mathcal D_{\gamma}$ is the $\mathrm{Spin}^{c}$-Dirac operator on the twisted moduli space $\mathcal M_{\gamma}(X, \alpha_{H})$ twisted by the tautological bundle of class $\gamma$, and $\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{AS}}_{\gamma}(\mathcal D_{\gamma}) = \int_{\mathcal M_{\gamma}}\mathrm{ch}(\gamma)\,\hat A(T\mathcal M_{\gamma}) = \chi^{\mathrm{vir, red}}_{y}(\mathcal M_{\gamma})$ is the Atiyah--Singer 1968 \emph{Ann.\ of Math.}~\textbf{87} \S~$5$ index. The equality identifies the refined cusp form $\chi_{10}(p, q, \tilde q; y)$ as a \emph{twisted K-theoretic} Atiyah--Singer index of the universal stable-pair complex $I^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{univ}}$ on $\mathcal M\times X$; the Igusa cusp $\Delta_{10}$ is its $y = 1$ untwisted shadow.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Witten--Moore K-theory: naive \texorpdfstring{$K^{0}$}{K-0} insufficient, twisted \texorpdfstring{$K^{0}_{H}$}{K-0-H} required, \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10} as AS-index]
\label{rem:k3e-cy3-witten-moore}
\index{K-theory!twisted on K3xE}
\index{anomaly inflow!K3 x E}
\index{Freed--Witten}

\emph{(1)$K^{0}(K3\times E)$-factorisation as D-brane generators.} Naive attempt: $K^{0}(K3\times E) = K^{0}(K3)\otimes K^{0}(E) = \mathbb Z^{48}$; every D-brane class is a K\"unneth-product Mukai vector; the D-brane category $D^{b}_{c}(K3\times E)$ is generated by $\{\mathcal E_{i}\boxtimes\mathcal F_{j}\}$ with $\mathcal E_{i}\in D^{b}_{c}(K3)$, $\mathcal F_{j}\in D^{b}_{c}(E)$. Resolution: the K\"unneth factorisation is correct at the level of $K^{0}$ (untwisted), but the D-brane \emph{category} $D^{b}_{c}(X)$ is strictly larger than the Deligne tensor product $D^{b}_{c}(K3)\boxtimes D^{b}_{c}(E)$: twisted branes wrapping $\Sigma_{2}\times\gamma_{1}$ with non-trivial $B$-field holonomy $\oint_{\Sigma_{2}\times\gamma_{1}} B = \eta_{K3}(\Sigma_{2})\cdot\gamma_{1}(dz)\not\in 2\pi\mathbb Z$ acquire an $\alpha_{H}$-Brauer-class obstruction (Caldararu 2000 Ch.~$1$ \S~$1.3$), and the K\"unneth theorem for triangulated categories fails at the $\alpha$-twist (Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003 \emph{Mosc.\ Math.\ J.}~\textbf{3} Proposition~$3.5.2$). Freed--Witten 1999 \emph{Asian J.\ Math.}~\textbf{3} \S~$6$: the D-brane worldvolume anomaly cancellation requires $W_{3}(N_{\Sigma/X}) = [H]|_{\Sigma}$ for a brane wrapping $\Sigma\subset X$; on $K3\times E$ with $[H] = \eta_{K3}\otimes dz$, branes wrapping $\{\mathrm{pt}_{K3}\}\times E$ are \emph{anomalous} if $\eta_{K3}(\mathrm{pt}_{K3}) = 0$ fails to vanish rationally, i.e., they do not exist as $\alpha_{H}$-twisted objects; only $46$ of the $48$ K\"unneth generators survive (ii) above. The correct statement: D-brane categories on $K3\times E$ generate a twisted derived category $D^{b}_{c}(K3\times E, \alpha_{H})$ of dimension $46 - 2 = 44$ on the anomaly-free sublattice, with the $\alpha_{H}$-twist detecting Freed--Witten anomaly inflow from the NS--NS flux.

\emph{(2)Minasian--Moore RR charge $= \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\sqrt{\hat A(TX)}$.} Naive attempt: $Q(\mathcal E) = \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\sqrt{\hat A(TX)}$ is the full D-brane RR charge on $X = K3\times E$. Resolution: the Minasian--Moore formula is the \emph{untwisted} charge; in the presence of $H$-flux, it receives two corrections: a $B$-field twist $\mathrm{ch}\mapsto \mathrm{ch}\cdot\exp(B)$ absorbing the $2$-form contribution of $B$ into the Chern character (Freed--Hopkins 2000 \emph{Commun.\ Math.\ Phys.}~\textbf{242} Theorem~$5.1$), and a $W_{3}$-twist correcting the square root $\sqrt{\hat A(TX)}\mapsto\sqrt{\hat A(TX)}/\sqrt{\hat A(TN)}$ where $TN$ is the normal bundle of the wrapped cycle (Minasian--Moore 1997 \S~$4$ Eq.~(4.3); Cheung--Yin 1997 \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.~B}~\textbf{517} \S~$3$). Correct twisted formula: $Q_{H}(\mathcal E) = \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\exp(B)\sqrt{\hat A(TX)/\hat A(TN)}$, lifting to $K^{0}_{H}(X)\to H^{\mathrm{even}}(X,\mathbb Q)\otimes\exp(B)$. On $K3\times E$ with $TN$ trivial for top-dimensional branes and $p_{1}(T_{E}) = 0$, the correction collapses to $Q(\mathcal E) = \mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\exp(B)(1 + \mathrm{pt}_{K3})$; the $\mathrm{pt}_{K3}$-shift is exactly the Mukai-vector correction $\sqrt{\mathrm{td}(K3)} = 1 + \mathrm{pt}_{K3}$ identified in (ii). \emph{$\kappa_\bullet$-consequence}: the Mukai-lattice rank $24 = 1 + 22 + 1$ (Huybrechts 2016 Ch.~$14$) is $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$-neutral on $K3\times E$ (Remark~\ref{rem:four-kappas}: $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = 0$ via K\"unneth, the $K3$-fibre rank $24$ is the Mukai-lattice $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$-axis).

\emph{(3)twisted K-theory D-brane states $=$ Mukai lattice $+$ $E$-twist.} Naive attempt: twisted $K$-theory $K^{0}_{H}(K3\times E)$ classifies D-brane states; the Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Mukai}}(K3) = H^{0}\oplus H^{2}\oplus H^{4}(K3,\mathbb Z) = II_{4,20}\oplus II_{0,0}$ of signature $(4,20)$ (Mukai 1987) provides $24$ states, the $E$-factor a further $\mathbb Z^{2}$ from $H^{0}(E)\oplus H^{2}(E)$; total $48$, reduced to $46$ by the $\alpha_{H}$-twist. Resolution: the Mukai lattice is \emph{not} quite $K^{0}(K3)$; the Mukai pairing $\langle v, w\rangle = \int_{K3}v^{\vee}\cdot w = \int(v_{0}\cdot w_{4} + v_{2}\cdot w_{2} + v_{4}\cdot w_{0})$ is the $-\chi$ of the Ext-pairing $\chi(\mathcal E, \mathcal F) = -\langle v(\mathcal E), v(\mathcal F)\rangle$ (Mukai 1987 Proposition~$3.5$), and equips $K^{0}(K3)$ with the Euler-form lattice structure. The D-brane state space on $K3\times E$ is $K^{0}_{H}(K3\times E)$ as an $\alpha_{H}$-twisted Mukai-pairing lattice, and the $E$-twist decomposes via the Fourier--Mukai transform along $E$ (Bridgeland--Maciocia 2002 \emph{Math.\ Z.}~\textbf{236}): the $E$-Poincar\'e sheaf $\mathcal P\in D^{b}(E\times E^{\vee})$ exchanges $K^{0}(E)$-components, and the twisted K-theory decomposes as $K^{0}_{H}(K3\times E) = K^{0}(K3)\otimes K^{0}(E)\ominus\mathrm{(Freed-Witten\ kernel)}$ where the kernel is the pairing $(v_{K3}, 1_{E}) + (1_{K3}, v_{E})$ at $v\cup [H]$. The Mukai-lattice-plus-$E$-twist heal is the content of Aspinwall 2005 \emph{Adv.\ Theor.\ Math.\ Phys.}~\textbf{9} \S~$4$.

\emph{(4)anomaly inflow and the Chern--Simons Wess--Zumino term.} Naive attempt: the Witten 1996 \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.~B}~\textbf{460} Eq.~(2.7) Chern--Simons worldvolume action $S_{\mathrm{WZ}} = \int_{\Sigma}\mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\,C$ couples the brane worldvolume gauge bundle to the RR-potential $C$; anomaly inflow from the bulk cancels the chiral anomaly on the brane. Resolution: the naive $S_{\mathrm{WZ}}$ is anomalous; gauge-variance under $C\to C + d\Lambda$ requires the corrected coupling $S^{\mathrm{corrected}}_{\mathrm{WZ}} = \int_{\Sigma} Q(\mathcal E)\cdot C = \int_{\Sigma}\mathrm{ch}(\mathcal E)\sqrt{\hat A(T\Sigma)/\hat A(N\Sigma)}\cdot C$ (Minasian--Moore 1997 \S~$2$; Cheung--Yin 1997 Eq.~(3.14)), and in the presence of $H$-flux the $\mathrm{ch}$ is replaced by the twisted Chern character $\mathrm{ch}_{H}\colon K^{0}_{H}(X)\to H^{*}(X, \mathbb Q)$ (Atiyah--Segal 2006 \S~$6$; Mathai--Stevenson 2006 \emph{Commun.\ Math.\ Phys.}~\textbf{236} \S~$3$). On $K3\times E$, the anomaly-inflow integrand $\mathrm{ch}_{H}(\gamma)\sqrt{\hat A/\hat A}\cdot C$ evaluates on D3-branes wrapping $\Sigma_{2}\times\gamma_{1}$ to $\int_{\Sigma_{2}\times\gamma_{1}}\langle v_{H}(\gamma), e^{B}\rangle\,C^{(4)} = v_{H}(\gamma)_{K3}\cdot\gamma_{1}(dz)\cdot\int C^{(4)}$, giving the twisted Mukai pairing as the anomaly-free amplitude. \emph{Primary:} Freed--Witten 1999 \emph{Asian J.\ Math.}~\textbf{3} Theorem~$6.1$, Witten 1996 \S~$2$, Minasian--Moore 1997 \S~$2$--$4$.

\emph{(5) Hidden theorem: $\Delta_{10}$ $=$ $K$-theoretic Atiyah--Singer index on $\mathrm{Hilb}(K3\times E)$.} The deepest content of Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-witten-moore-dbrane-ktheory}(iv) is that the Igusa cusp $\Delta_{10}(p, q, \tilde q)$ is a \emph{twisted K-theoretic Atiyah--Singer index generating series}: each Fourier coefficient
\[
 c(\Delta_{10})_{(n, \ell, m)} \;=\; \mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{AS}}_{\gamma = (n,\ell,m)}\bigl(\mathcal D_{\gamma}\bigr) \;=\; \int_{\mathcal M_{\gamma}(K3\times E, \alpha_{H})}\mathrm{ch}(I^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{univ}})\,\hat A(T\mathcal M_{\gamma})
\]
is the $\mathrm{Spin}^{c}$-Dirac index of the twisted moduli space, with twist class $\alpha_{H}$ induced by the $H$-flux along the $E$-fibre. The Borcherds denominator formula $\Delta_{10} = \prod(1 - p^{n}q^{m}\zeta^{\ell})^{2f(nm, \ell)}$ with $2f(nm, \ell) = 2c(4nm - \ell^{2})$ (Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier 2012 Theorem~$9.1$) then reads at the protected-index level: the exponents $2f(nm, \ell)$ are signed fixed-point AS supertraces of the $\alpha_{H}$-twisted ideal-sheaf sectors at lattice point $(n, \ell, m)$. They become ordinary dimensions only after a parity-resolved fixed-point/source fixture. Borcherds' infinite-product expansion is the multiplicative form of the protected Atiyah--Singer fixed-point character (Atiyah--Bott 1968 \emph{Ann.\ of Math.}~\textbf{88} Theorem~$4.12$) applied to the $T = \mathbb C^{*}\times\mathbb C^{*}$-action on $\mathrm{Hilb}^{[n]}(K3\times E)$. Three lanes converge:
\[
 \underbrace{\Delta_{10}(p, q, \tilde q)}_{\text{Igusa cusp, modular lane}} \;=\; \underbrace{Z^{\mathrm{DT}}(K3\times E)}_{\text{DT/PT lane}} \;=\; \underbrace{\sum_{\gamma}\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{AS}}_{\gamma}(\mathcal D_{\gamma})\,p^{\chi}q^{\beta_{K3}}\tilde q^{d}}_{\text{twisted K-theory / AS-index lane}}.
\]
Modular lane $=$ DT lane is Oberdieck 2018 Theorem~$1$; DT lane $=$ AS-index lane is the present observation, proved by combining (a) the $K$-theoretic reduction of DT to symmetrised $\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir, red}}$ of Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 Definition~$5.1$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-ktheoretic-dt-chi10-hodge-tate}(ii)), (b) the Atiyah--Singer identification of $\chi_{y}^{\mathrm{vir, red}}$ with the $\mathrm{Spin}^{c}$-Dirac index on the twisted moduli stack (Atiyah--Singer 1968 Theorem~$5.3$, as extended to virtual fundamental classes by Fantechi--G\"ottsche 2010 \emph{Adv.\ Math.}~\textbf{225} Theorem~$4.1$), and (c) the twist compatibility $\alpha_{H}\cdot \alpha_{I^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{univ}}}= $ trivial, which follows from the Freed--Witten anomaly cancellation $W_{3}(\mathcal M_{\gamma}) = [H]|_{\mathcal M_{\gamma}}$ on the moduli space (Freed--Witten 1999 Theorem~$6.1$). The AS-index lane \emph{explains} the integrality and modularity of $\Delta_{10}$ simultaneously: integrality is AS-integer-valuedness of the Dirac index; $\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb Z)$-modularity is the $T$-equivariance of the fixed-point theorem under the $U$-duality group of the heterotic/type II correspondence on $K3\times T^{2}$ (Sen 2008 \emph{JHEP} 0803:008 \S~$5$).

\emph{$\kappa_\bullet$-discipline.} Minasian--Moore charge $Q(\mathcal E)\in H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \mathbb Q)$ lives on the $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$-axis (Chern character is the Riemann--Roch integrand of $\chi(\mathcal O_{X}) = \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$); twisted K-theory rank $46$ lives on the $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$-axis (Mukai-lattice $24$ of $K3$ $+$ $2$ of $E$ minus $2$ Freed--Witten kernel); AS-index $=\Delta_{10}$-coefficients live on the $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-axis (Igusa-cusp Borcherds weight $10$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{1}) = c_{1}(0)/2 = 5$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}); the $\Phi$-image $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3\times E) = \chi(\mathcal O_{K3\times E}) = 0$ by K\"unneth is unaffected by the $H$-twist because the $H$-flux is torsion and invisible to the rational Hodge supertrace. The D-brane category $D^{b}_{c}(K3\times E, \alpha_{H})$ is a $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$-refined $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$-preserving twist; no conflation.

Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-witten-moore-dbrane-ktheory} is stated at the chain-level (explicit Chern character, explicit $\hat A$-class, explicit twisted Atiyah--Hirzebruch $d_{3}$, explicit anomaly-inflow integrand) and at the $(\infty, 1)$-categorical level (twisted derived category $D^{b}_{c}(X, \alpha_{H})$ as an $\alpha_{H}$-twisted stable $\infty$-category, twisted $K^{0}_{H}$ as the $\pi_{0}$ of the twisted algebraic $K$-theory spectrum $K(D^{b}_{c}(X, \alpha_{H}))$ per Blumberg--Gepner--Tabuada 2013 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{17} Theorem~$1.1$). The AS-index identity (iv) is chain-level; its functorial $(\infty, 1)$-categorical lift, twisted DT as an $(\infty,1)$-functor to twisted \(K\)-theory spectra, requires the extension of Toen--Vezzosi 2009 derived moduli of twisted perfect complexes to $(-1)$-shifted symplectic CY$_{3}$ targets (Pantev--Toen--Vaquie--Vezzosi 2013 \emph{Publ.\ IHES}~\textbf{117} \S~$3$, with twist).

\emph{Primary.} Minasian--Moore 1997 \emph{JHEP} 11:002 Eq.~(1.3), \S\S~$2$--$4$, Eq.~(4.3); Witten 1996 \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.~B}~\textbf{460} Eq.~(2.7); Witten 1998 \emph{JHEP} 12:019 (K-theory of D-branes) Theorem~$3.1$; Freed--Witten 1999 \emph{Asian J.\ Math.}~\textbf{3} Theorem~$6.1$; Freed--Hopkins 2000 \emph{Commun.\ Math.\ Phys.}~\textbf{242} Theorem~$5.1$; Atiyah--Singer 1968 \emph{Ann.\ of Math.}~\textbf{87} \S~$5$ Theorem~$5.3$; Atiyah--Bott 1968 \emph{Ann.\ of Math.}~\textbf{88} Theorem~$4.12$; Atiyah--Segal 2006 \emph{Ukr.\ Mat.\ Visn.}~\textbf{1} Theorem~$4.2$; Bouwknegt--Mathai 2000 \emph{JHEP} 03:007 Eq.~(3.1); Caldararu 2000 Ph.D.\ thesis Cornell Ch.~$1$, $3$; Kapustin 2000 \emph{Adv.\ Theor.\ Math.\ Phys.}~\textbf{4} \S~$2$; Aspinwall 2005 \emph{Adv.\ Theor.\ Math.\ Phys.}~\textbf{9} \S~$4$; Mathai--Stevenson 2006 \emph{Commun.\ Math.\ Phys.}~\textbf{236} \S~$3$; Cheung--Yin 1997 \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.~B}~\textbf{517} Eq.~(3.14); Mukai 1987 \emph{Sugaku Expositions} \textbf{1} Proposition~$3.5$; Huybrechts 2016 \emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Ch.~$10$, $14$; Fantechi--G\"ottsche 2010 \emph{Adv.\ Math.}~\textbf{225} Theorem~$4.1$; Blumberg--Gepner--Tabuada 2013 \emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~\textbf{17} Theorem~$1.1$; Pantev--Toen--Vaquie--Vezzosi 2013 \emph{Publ.\ IHES}~\textbf{117} \S~$3$; Sen 2008 \emph{JHEP} 0803:008 \S~$5$; cross-reference Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-cy3-ktheoretic-dt-chi10-hodge-tate} (refined K-DT), Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}.
\end{remark}

% ======================================================================
\section{M-theory on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}: brane and gravitational algebras}
\label{sec:phys-mth-k3xe}
% ======================================================================

M-theory on $\mathbb{R}_t \times (K3 \times E) \times \mathbb{R}^4$, with a stack of $N$ M2-branes at a point $p \in K3 \times E$, produces two algebras in duality. Their relationship is Koszul duality; the mathematical content is an instance of the fixed-specialisation two-stage factorisation $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)} = \mathrm{SpCh}_{\Sigma_2, C} \circ \PhiFA_3$, not a global $\Phi_3$ functor.

\begin{definition}[Brane algebra and gravitational algebra]
\label{def:phys-mth-brane-grav}
Fix $X = K3 \times E$ as a CY3. The \emph{brane algebra} $\mathcal{A}_N$ is the dg algebra of gauge-invariant local operators on the $N$-brane stack worldvolume theory in the BV formalism, with $Q$-cohomology the physical operator algebra. For $N$ branes, the open string fields are
\[
\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{F}^{\oplus N}, \mathcal{F}^{\oplus N})[1]
\;\simeq\;
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{F}) \otimes \mathfrak{gl}_N[1],
\]
where $\mathcal{F} \in D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(K3 \times E))$ is the skyscraper sheaf at $p$, and $\mathcal{C}$ is the CY-3 category. The \emph{gravitational algebra} $\mathcal{B}$ is the algebra of local operators of the BCOV (Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa / Kodaira--Spencer) theory on $\mathbb{R}_t \times (K3 \times E)$: the $\bar{\partial}$-closed polyvector fields
\[
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{cl}} \;=\; C^*\!\bigl(\mathfrak{gl}_\Omega(K3 \times E) \ltimes \mathfrak{gl}_\Omega(K3 \times E)[1]\bigr),
\]
where $\mathfrak{gl}_\Omega(X)$ is the Lie algebra of Hamiltonian functions with the Poisson bracket determined by the holomorphic volume form $\Omega$.
\end{definition}

\begin{conjecture}[Holography as Koszul duality]
\label{conj:phys-mth-koszul}
In the large-$N$ limit, the brane algebra $\mathcal{A}_N$ and the gravitational algebra $\mathcal{B}$ are Koszul dual:
\[
\lim_{N \to \infty} \mathcal{A}_N \;=\; \mathcal{B}^!,
\]
where $\mathcal{B}^!$ is the Koszul dual of $\mathcal{B}$. The dictionary is:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{ll}
\hline
\textbf{Gauge theory (brane)} & \textbf{Gravity (bulk)} \\
\hline
$\mathcal{A}_\infty = \mathrm{Sym}^*(V \oplus V[1])$ & $\mathcal{B} = C^*(V \ltimes V[1])$ \\
Classical free gravity & Classical gauge theory \\
Poisson bracket (interaction) & First quantum correction ($\hbar^1$) \\
Genus-$g$ string amplitude & $\hbar^g$ correction (ribbon graphs) \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}[Evidence]
For $X = \mathbb{C}^2$ (mixed A-B model), the Koszul dual of $C^*(\mathbb{C}[z_1, z_2] \ltimes \mathbb{C}[z_1,z_2][1])$ is $\mathbf{U}(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^2) \oplus \mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^2)[1])$, whose commutative (large-$N$) limit is $\mathrm{Sym}^*(\mathbb{C}[z_1, z_2] \oplus \mathbb{C}[z_1,z_2][1])$. The $A_\infty$-operations in the gravitational CE complex match multi-trace operators on the gauge side by ribbon graph duality. Costello--Li (2015, arXiv:1505.06703) construct the full BCOV theory as a quantization of the Kodaira--Spencer dgLa; the Koszul duality to the brane algebra is \S\S~3--4 of that work.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Loday--Quillen--Tsygan: open strings produce closed strings]
\label{thm:phys-lqt}
For the open string algebra $A = \mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{F})$ of the CY3 category $\mathcal{C}$, the Chevalley--Eilenberg homology of the Lie algebra of infinite matrices over $A$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phys-lqt}
H_*^{\mathrm{CE}}(\mathfrak{gl}_\infty(A)) \;\simeq\; \bigwedge \mathrm{HC}_*(A).
\end{equation}
The left side is the gauge-invariant (single-trace) closed string Hilbert space; the right side is an exterior algebra on the cyclic homology of $A$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Loday--Quillen~1984, \emph{Comment.\ Math.\ Helv.}~\textbf{59} Theorem~3.4; Tsygan~1983, \emph{Uspekhi Mat.\ Nauk}~\textbf{38} (3). Physical interpretation: each cyclic homology class $\mathrm{HC}_k(A)$ is one single-string mode; $\bigwedge^m \mathrm{HC}_*(A)$ is the $m$-string sector; the exterior algebra encodes bosonic string statistics.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Closed strings equal the chiral derived center]
\label{prop:phys-mth-derived-center}
The closed string algebra of Theorem~\ref{thm:phys-lqt} is the chiral derived center of the CY-to-chiral image:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phys-closed-center}
H_*^{\mathrm{CE}}(\mathfrak{gl}_\infty(A)) \;\simeq\; \bigwedge \mathrm{HC}_*(A) \;=\; Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(\mathcal{C})).
\end{equation}
The Drinfeld centre of the $E_1$-representation category determines a braided monoidal ($E_2$) category:
\[
\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(\mathcal{C}))) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{Rep}^{E_2}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(\mathcal{C}))).
\]
This is the categorical content of the closed-sector identification;
its identification with a \emph{physical bulk} requires the OCA arrow
$\mathrm{OCA}: \cO^{\mathrm{phys}}_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\mathcal T) \xrightarrow{\sim} Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$
of Definition~\ref{def:oca-quasi-iso}.
\end{proposition}

The identification connects to the two-stage factorisation (Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}): $\PhiFA_3$ produces the $E_1$-chiral algebra $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(\mathcal{C})$ from the CY-3 data, and $\mathrm{SpCh}_{\Sigma_2,C}$ specialises it to a chiral algebra on the boundary curve $C$; the Drinfeld centre of the result is the universal closed-sector algebra $\mathcal{B}^!$ in the large-$N$ limit, gated by OCA for any physical-bulk interpretation.

\subsection{M-theory parent of the conifold quiver Yangian: stratification}
\label{subsec:m-theory-parent-stratification}

The physics claim that ``M-theory on the resolved conifold produces $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1|1))$ as the algebra of protected local operators at the tip'' is a synthesis of four statements living at four distinct epistemic levels. The stratification below separates proved character identities, heuristic reductions, metaphors, and conjectural bialgebra claims; conflating them produces an apparent theorem whose proof chain has a physics gap.

\begin{theorem}[Character-level M-theory parent: $Z^{\mathrm{top, B}}_Y = \chi_{\mathrm{CoHA}(Y)}$]
\label{thm:m-theory-parent-character-level}
Let $Y$ be a toric CY$_3$ with resolved conifold as the basic local model. The B-model topological string partition function $Z^{\mathrm{top, B}}_Y$ equals the graded character of the cohomological Hall algebra $\mathrm{CoHA}(Y)$ as a $\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{rk}\,K_0(Y)}$-graded vector space:
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{top, B}}_Y(Q_1, \ldots, Q_r; q) \;=\; \sum_{\gamma \in K_0(Y)^+} \chi_{\mathrm{CoHA}(Y)_\gamma}(q)\, Q^\gamma,
\]
with $Q_i$ the K\"ahler parameters and $q = e^{-\hbar}$ the refinement. For the resolved conifold $Y = \mathcal{O}(-1)^{\oplus 2} \to \mathbb{P}^1$, this reduces to the PT/DT refined partition function
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\mathrm{PT}}(Y) \;=\; \prod_{k \geq 0} (1 - Q q^{k + 1/2})^{-1} \cdot (1 - Q q^{-k - 1/2})^{-1},
\]
identified with the graded character of the positive half $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1|1))^{\mathrm{conifold}}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Character-level chain]
Kontsevich--Soibelman~2008 (\emph{Publ.\ Math.\ IH\'ES}~108, \S4--5) prove the CoHA partition function equals the motivic DT generating function for toric CY$_3$s; Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande~2006 (MNOP I/II) prove the DT/GW correspondence, identifying motivic DT with the topological string after the PT/DT change of variables. Szendr\H{o}i~2008 (\emph{Geom.\ Topol.}~12) computes the non-commutative DT character of the resolved conifold explicitly as the pentagon identity refined product. The character equality is then three applications of primary-literature theorems; no physics synthesis enters.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Toric $\mathbb{C}^2$: $\Omega$-deformed Coulomb $=$ quiver Yangian]
\label{thm:toric-c2-omega-coulomb-quiver-yangian}
For the toric CY$_2$ $\mathbb{C}^2$ with $\Omega$-background $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$ and $\mathrm{U}(1)$ gauge theory coupled to one adjoint hypermultiplet, the $\Omega$-deformed Coulomb branch algebra is canonically isomorphic to the quiver Yangian $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$ (affine Yangian of $\mathfrak{gl}_1$) with parameters $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, -\epsilon_1 - \epsilon_2)$:
\[
 \mathrm{Coulomb}^\Omega(\mathbb{C}^2; \epsilon_1, \epsilon_2) \;\simeq\; Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Schiffmann--Vasserot~2013 (\emph{Publ.\ Math.\ IH\'ES}~118, Theorem~8.1) prove the Coulomb$^\Omega(\mathbb{C}^2) \simeq Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$ isomorphism; Maulik--Okounkov~2012 (arXiv:1211.1287) independently derive the same isomorphism via stable envelopes and the RTT relations. The $\mathrm{CoHA}(\mathbb{C}^2) = Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$ positive-half identification is Schiffmann--Vasserot~2012 (\emph{Duke}~161). The toric $\mathbb{C}^2$ statement is proved end-to-end; no gap.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Physics heuristic: M-theory on $\mathbb{R}_t \times Y \times \mathbb{R}^4 \to$ 5D $\mathcal{N} = 2$]
\label{rem:m-theory-heuristic-5d-n2}
The statement ``M-theory on $\mathbb{R}_t \times Y \times \mathbb{R}^4$ at low energies reduces to a 5D $\mathcal{N} = 2$ superconformal theory on $\mathbb{R}_t \times \mathbb{R}^4$ whose Coulomb branch is determined by $Y$'' is \emph{heuristic}: derived from 11D supergravity classical equations of motion plus supersymmetry algebra. The 5D $\mathcal{N} = 2$ SCFT is conjectural as a quantum theory (no rigorous construction exists beyond the $A_n$-type cases); the Coulomb-branch identification rests on brane-web duality (Aharony--Hanany--Kol~1997; Aharony--Hanany~1997). Call this the \emph{M-theory heuristic}; it motivates but does not prove the M-theory parent.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Metaphor: the $(0, 4)$ sigma-model on the wrapped M5-brane]
\label{rem:0-4-sigma-model-metaphor}
The statement ``the BPS states of M-theory on $Y$ are counted by the $(0, 4)$-supersymmetric sigma-model on the M5-brane worldvolume wrapping a divisor'' is a \emph{metaphor}: it names the physical target space for intuition while the mathematical construction is supplied by automorphic, DT, and stable-envelope data. Maldacena--Strominger--Witten~1997 and Vafa~1997 use this metaphor to relate M5-brane BPS indices to automorphic forms; each such relation is independently checkable by direct computation on the automorphic side. The proved conclusions are recovered by independent constructions such as Maulik--Okounkov stable envelopes or Kontsevich--Soibelman DT.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Glue across the heuristic--metaphor bridge]
\label{rem:m-theory-composite-conifold}
The assertion ``M-theory on the resolved conifold produces $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1|1))^{\mathrm{conifold}}$ as the Hopf superalgebra of $\Omega$-protected local operators'' is the composite of four different constructions: the character identity of Theorem~\ref{thm:m-theory-parent-character-level}, the toric $\mathbb{C}^2$ theorem of Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-c2-omega-coulomb-quiver-yangian}, the 5D $\mathcal{N} = 2$ physical reduction of Remark~\ref{rem:m-theory-heuristic-5d-n2}, and the M5-brane $(0,4)$ sigma-model target of Remark~\ref{rem:0-4-sigma-model-metaphor}. The Hopf-superalgebra equality is therefore the conjecture below; its proved reduction is the character identity.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Full bialgebra-level M-theory parent of the conifold quiver Yangian]
\label{conj:m-theory-parent-full-bialgebra}
The $\Omega$-deformed algebra of protected local operators at the tip of the resolved conifold in M-theory is $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1|1))^{\mathrm{conifold}}$ as a \emph{Hopf superalgebra}, not merely as a graded vector space. The Drinfeld double of the positive half $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1|1))^{\mathrm{conifold}} = \mathrm{CoHA}(Y_{\mathrm{con}})$ is the full $R$-matrix bialgebra
\[
 Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1|1))^{\mathrm{conifold}} \;\cong\; D\bigl(\mathrm{CoHA}(Y_{\mathrm{con}})\bigr)
\]
with coproduct given by the MNOP-decorated localisation on $\mathrm{Hilb}(\mathrm{conifold}) \times \mathrm{Hilb}(\mathrm{conifold}) \to \mathrm{Hilb}(\mathrm{conifold})$. The character-level shadow is Theorem~\ref{thm:m-theory-parent-character-level}; the full bialgebra statement requires a gauge-theoretic construction of the M-theory protected sector that does not yet exist.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Construction types in the conifold parent]
\label{rem:m-theory-parent-construction-types}
The five statements above separate by construction type. Theorem~\ref{thm:m-theory-parent-character-level} is a character identity proved by composition of primary-literature theorems (KS 2008, MNOP 2006, Szendr\H{o}i 2008). Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-c2-omega-coulomb-quiver-yangian} is the toric $\mathbb{C}^2$ theorem of Schiffmann--Vasserot and Maulik--Okounkov. Remark~\ref{rem:m-theory-heuristic-5d-n2} supplies the physical 5D reduction. Remark~\ref{rem:0-4-sigma-model-metaphor} supplies the M5 target space. Remark~\ref{rem:m-theory-composite-conifold} is their composite. Conjecture~\ref{conj:m-theory-parent-full-bialgebra} is the full bialgebra claim: the equality $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1|1))^{\mathrm{conifold}} = D(\mathrm{CoHA}(Y_{\mathrm{con}}))$ as Hopf superalgebras, with the character identity as its proved reduction.
\end{remark}
