\section{The BCOV holomorphic anomaly as Maurer--Cartan equation}
\label{sec:phys-top-bcov-mc}
% ======================================================================

The B-model holomorphic anomaly equation of Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa~1994 is identified with the Maurer--Cartan equation of the shadow obstruction tower.

\begin{proposition}[BCOV = MC, schematic]
\label{prop:phys-bcov-mc}
Let $X^\vee$ be the mirror CY3 with complex structure moduli space $\mathcal{M}$. The genus-$g$ free energy $F_g$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phys-bcov}
\partial_{\bar{i}} F_g \;=\; \frac{1}{2}\bar{C}^{jk}_{\bar{i}}
\!\left(D_j D_k F_{g-1} + \sum_{r=1}^{g-1} D_j F_r\, D_k F_{g-r}\right), \quad g \geq 2,
\end{equation}
where $D_i = \nabla_i + (2-2g)\partial_i K$ and $\bar{C}^{jk}_{\bar{i}}$ is the anti-holomorphic Yukawa coupling. This is the Maurer--Cartan equation
\[
D\Theta + \tfrac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0
\]
in the dgLa $\mathcal{L}_X = \mathrm{PV}^{\bullet,\bullet}(X^\vee)$ of polyvector-valued $(0,q)$-forms, expanded at order $\hbar^{2g-2}$. The total free energy $\Theta = \sum_g \hbar^{2g-2} \Theta^{(g)}$ (with $\Theta^{(g)} \sim F_g$) is a MC element; the propagator $S^{ij}$ of special geometry is the Lie bracket contraction kernel; the Yukawa coupling $C_{ijk}$ is the Schouten--Nijenhuis structure constant; holomorphic ambiguity of $F_g$ is MC gauge freedom. In particular, $\mathcal{L}_X$ is quasi-isomorphic to the dgLa $C^\bullet(B(A_X))$ governing deformations of the bar complex of $G(X)$: the BCOV MC element $\Theta$ is the shadow obstruction tower $\Theta_{A_X}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
BCOV~1994, \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.\ B}~\textbf{405} Eq.~(3.22), gives the anomaly equation. Costello--Li~2015 arXiv:1505.06703 \S~3 gives the MC interpretation in $\mathcal L_X$. The Hochschild/bar comparison identifies $\mathcal L_X$ with $C^\bullet(B(A_X))$, carrying the BCOV MC element into the chiral shadow complex.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Invariant discipline at genus 1]
At genus $g=1$, the anomaly is $\partial_{\bar{i}}\partial_j F_1 = \tfrac{1}{2}C_{jkl}\bar{C}^{kl}_{\bar{i}} - (\chi(X)/24 - 1)G_{j\bar{i}}$, where $\chi(X)$ is the Euler characteristic. The coefficient $\chi(X)/24$ governs the genus-1 gravitational anomaly; it need not equal a chiral modular characteristic. For $K3 \times E$, $\chi = 0$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = 0$ by K\"{u}nneth. The compact total-space Hodge supertrace is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3\times E)=0$, the Heisenberg Stage-$2$ branch has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}=3$, and the Borcherds denominator has the separate weight $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=\left.c_N(0)/2\right|_{N=1}=5$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}. The equality $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}=\chi(X)/24$ is a compact-total-space Hodge-supertrace statement here; it is not the Heisenberg branch and not the BKM weight.
\end{remark}

% ======================================================================
\section{The topological vertex as local root datum}
\label{sec:phys-top-vertex}
% ======================================================================

For toric CY3, the A-model partition function factorizes over trivalent vertices of the toric diagram. Each vertex carries the positive-half local CoHA datum
$Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$; the Drinfeld double gives the affine Yangian, and the $\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}$ object appears only after the centre/vacuum evaluation.

\begin{construction}[AKMV topological vertex and local root datum]
\label{constr:phys-top-vertex-root}
The A-model partition function on any toric CY3 $X$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phys-top-vertex-Z}
Z(X) \;=\; \sum_{\{\lambda_e\}} \prod_{v}\, C_{\lambda_{e_1}\lambda_{e_2}\lambda_{e_3}}(q)
\cdot \prod_{e} (-Q_e)^{|\lambda_e|} f_{\lambda_e}(q),
\end{equation}
where the sum is over assignments of partitions to internal edges, $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q)$ is the topological vertex
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phys-c-vertex}
C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q) \;=\; q^{\kappa_\mu/2}\, s_{\nu^t}(q^\rho)
\sum_\eta s_{\lambda^t/\eta}(q^{\nu+\rho})\, s_{\mu/\eta}(q^{\nu^t+\rho}),
\end{equation}
and $Q_e = e^{-t_e}$ is the K\"{a}hler parameter of the edge. The local root datum at vertex $v$ is the positive-half CoHA datum of $\mathbb{C}^3$: the lattice is $\mathbb{Z}$ (dimension vectors), root multiplicities are $\mathrm{mult}(n)=p(n)$ (plane partitions), and $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q)$ is the matrix element of the topological-vertex intertwiner on Fock spaces. Passing from this positive half to the full quantum vertex chiral group requires the Drinfeld double/centre construction. With that extra datum, the global root datum $\mathcal{R}(X)$ is assembled by tensor product (factorization product over the toric diagram): the edge sum~\eqref{eq:phys-top-vertex-Z} is the trace over $\bigotimes_e \mathcal{F}_e$ with insertion of vertex operators and K\"{a}hler weights.
\end{construction}

\begin{remark}[MacMahon function and the denominator identity]
The MacMahon function $M(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}$ is the DT partition function of $\mathbb{C}^3$ and the vacuum character obtained from the Drinfeld-double/centre evaluation of the affine-Yangian side. It is not a statement that the positive CoHA itself is $\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}$: the positive half is $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$, the full double is $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$, and the vertex-algebra vacuum character is the centre/vacuum shadow. For $K3 \times E$ (non-toric), the denominator identity is the Igusa cusp form $\Delta_5$, and the appropriate analogue of the topological vertex is the BKM denominator product form.
\end{remark}

% ======================================================================
\section{Celestial holography and quantum vertex chiral groups}
\label{sec:phys-cel-holography}
% ======================================================================

The Mellin transform of 4d massless scattering amplitudes in $\mathbb{R}^{1,3}$ yields 2d celestial amplitudes on the celestial sphere $S^2 \cong \mathbb{P}^1$. The holomorphic sector is a vertex algebra whose OPE encodes collinear singularities of the S-matrix. For 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ theories arising from Type IIB on a CY3 $X$, identifying this celestial chiral algebra with a genus-$0$ sector of $G(X)$ requires both a constructed $G(X)$ and the pure mathematical holographic bridge datum.

\begin{theorem}[Triple identification for flat space]
\label{thm:phys-cel-triple-id}
There is a canonical positive-half/double/vacuum chain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phys-triple-id}
\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}\text{-vac} \;\curvearrowleft\; Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)=D(Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)) \;\simeq\; G(\mathbb{C}^3),
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}$ is the unique one-parameter family of vertex algebras freely generated by one current in each spin $s \geq 1$, $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)=D(Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1))$ is the Drinfeld double of the positive half, and $G(\mathbb{C}^3)$ is the quantum vertex chiral group of the toric CY3 $\mathbb{C}^3$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
The affine Yangian action on the $\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}$ vacuum module is the Maulik--Okounkov/Schiffmann--Vasserot construction on equivariant cohomology of $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\mathbb{C}^2)$. Theorem~\ref{thm:sv-c3} identifies the critical CoHA of the Jordan quiver with cubic potential $W = \mathrm{tr}(XYZ - XZY)$ as $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$; the full affine Yangian, hence $G(\mathbb{C}^3)$, is the Drinfeld double $D(Y^+)$, not the CoHA itself.
\end{proof}

The physical content of~\eqref{eq:phys-triple-id}: the celestial symmetry algebra of 4d gravity ($\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}$, established by Guevara--Himwich--Pate--Strominger~2021) is the quantum vertex chiral group of flat space ($G(\mathbb{C}^3)$). The infinite tower of higher-spin celestial symmetries $W^{(s)}$ for $s \geq 1$ is the tower of BPS states in the DT theory of $\mathbb{C}^3$.

\begin{conjecture}[CY3 celestial chiral algebra]
\label{conj:phys-cy3-celestial}
Assume $G(X)$ has been constructed from a framed CY-to-chiral datum and
the pure mathematical holographic bridge datum is supplied. For a 4d
$\mathcal{N}=2$ theory $T_X$ from Type IIB on a CY3 $X$, the celestial
chiral algebra is then expected to be
\[
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{cel}}(T_X) \;\simeq\; G(X)^{g=0},
\]
the genus-0 (tree-level) sector of $G(X)$, with higher-genus corrections from the shadow obstruction tower $\Theta_{A_X}$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proposition}[Soft theorems and the shadow filtration]
\label{prop:phys-cel-soft-shadow}
Assume \(G(X)\) has been constructed and the pure mathematical
holographic bridge datum has been supplied.
The soft graviton theorems are identified with the degree filtration of the shadow obstruction tower only after the celestial algebra is matched with the constructed boundary chiral algebra. Under that bridge, the $\mathrm{sub}^k$-leading soft theorem at $\mathcal{O}(\omega^{k-1})$ is the Ward identity of the degree-$(k+2)$ shadow $\Theta^{(k+2)}_{A_X}$:
\[
\mathrm{Sh}_{0,n}\bigl(\Theta^{(k+2)}_{A_X}\bigr) \cdot \Phi = 0
\qquad \text{(degree-$(k+2)$ soft Ward identity)}.
\]
The dictionary is:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{llll}
\hline
\textit{Degree} & \textit{Shadow} & \textit{Soft theorem} & \textit{Celestial current} \\
\hline
$r=2$ & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$ & Weinberg leading & Supertranslation \\
$r=3$ & $\mathfrak{C}(A_X)$ & Cachazo--Strominger subleading & Superrotation (Virasoro) \\
$r=4$ & $\mathfrak{Q}(A_X)$ & Sub-subleading & Spin-3 $\mathcal{W}$-current \\
$r$ & $\Theta^{(r)}_{A_X}$ & $\mathrm{Sub}^{r-2}$-leading & Spin-$(r-1)$ $\mathcal{W}$-current \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
For $X = \mathbb{C}^3$ with $r_{\max} = \infty$, the full tower generates $\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}$, recovering Guevara--Himwich--Pate--Strominger~2021.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution and bridge condition]
Leading soft graviton: Weinberg~1965. Subleading: Cachazo--Strominger~2014. Celestial algebra: Strominger~2014; He--Lysov--Mitra--Strominger~2015. The shadow tower identification follows only after the pure bridge identifies the celestial Ward algebra with the boundary chiral algebra; inside that conditional target, the degree decomposition of the modular shadow connection gives the displayed filtration.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Celestial OPE as collision \texorpdfstring{$r$}{r}-matrix]
\label{conj:phys-cel-ope-r-matrix}
When $G(X)$ is constructed, the celestial OPE of the 4d theory $T_X$ is determined by its collision $r$-matrix:
\[
\mathcal{O}_{\Delta_1}(z_1) \cdot \mathcal{O}_{\Delta_2}(z_2)
\;\sim\;
r_{G(X)}(z_1 - z_2) \cdot (\mathcal{O}_{\Delta_1} \otimes \mathcal{O}_{\Delta_2})(z_2),
\]
where $r_{G(X)}(z) = \mathrm{Res}^{\mathrm{coll}}_{0,2}(\Theta_{A_X})$ is the degree-2 genus-0 projection of $\Theta_{A_X}$. The number of singular terms in the OPE equals $r_{\max}(G(X)) - 1$; the leading simple pole coefficient is proportional to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$; the Yang--Baxter equation for $r_{G(X)}(z)$ is the associativity of the celestial OPE.
\end{conjecture}

% ======================================================================
\section{3d mirror symmetry and \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}_2$}{CY-2} quantum vertex chiral groups}
\label{sec:phys-3dm-cy2}
% ======================================================================

A 3d $\mathcal{N}=4$ gauge theory $T$ has two hyperk\"{a}hler branches of vacua: the Higgs branch $M_H(T) = T^*R \mathbin{/\!\!/\!\!/} G$ (exact, non-renormalized) and the Coulomb branch $M_C(T)$ (quantum-corrected). Both are $\mathrm{CY}_2$ (hyperk\"{a}hler of complex dimension 2): the functor $\Phi$ (at $d=2$) produces $E_2$-chiral algebras from their derived categories. Mirror symmetry exchanges the two branches and, conjecturally, their quantum vertex chiral groups.

\begin{theorem}[Hyperk\"{a}hler branches are \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{CY}_2$}{CY-2}]
\label{thm:phys-3dm-hk}
Both $M_H(T)$ and $M_C(T)$ carry hyperk\"{a}hler metrics. A hyperk\"{a}hler surface (complex dimension 2, holonomy $\subseteq \mathrm{Sp}(1) \cong \mathrm{SU}(2)$) is a $\mathrm{CY}_2$ space. Consequently $D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(M_H))$ and $D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(M_C))$ are both $\mathrm{CY}_2$ categories; the $\mathbb{S}^2$-framing of $\mathrm{HH}_\bullet$ provides the $E_2$-algebra structure on their $\Phi$-images.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Higgs branch: hyperk\"{a}hler quotient theorem of Hitchin--Karlhede--Lindstr\"{o}m--Ro\v{c}ek~1987. Coulomb branch: Seiberg--Witten~1996 analysis of the 3d low-energy effective action; Braverman--Finkelberg--Nakajima~2016--2019 rigorous construction via convolution on the affine Grassmannian.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[BFN Coulomb branch algebra]
\label{def:phys-bfn}
For a complex reductive group $G$ and representation $R$, the Coulomb branch algebra is
\[
\mathcal{A}_{G,R} \;=\; H^{G(\!(t)\!)}_\bullet(\mathcal{R}), \qquad
\mathcal{R} = \{(g,s) \mid g \in \mathrm{Gr}_G,\; s \in R[\![t]\!],\; g\cdot s \in R[\![t]\!]\},
\]
with convolution product. The Coulomb branch is $M_C(G,R) = \mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{A}_{G,R})$. Its quantization $\mathcal{A}^\hbar_{G,R} = K^{G[\![t]\!]}_\bullet(\mathcal{R})$ is a filtered associative algebra.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[CoHA = Yangian positive half]
\label{thm:phys-3dm-coha-yangian}
For a quiver $Q$ without potential (the preprojective algebra case), the cohomological Hall algebra of the Higgs branch Nakajima quiver variety is
\[
\mathrm{CoHA}(Q) \;\cong\; Y^+(\mathfrak{g}_Q),
\]
the positive half of the Yangian of the Kac--Moody algebra $\mathfrak{g}_Q$. The passage from the associative ($E_1$) CoHA to the braided monoidal ($E_2$) full quantum vertex chiral group $G(M_H)$ requires the Maulik--Okounkov $R$-matrix from chamber wall-crossing on Nakajima varieties via stable envelopes.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
$\mathrm{CoHA}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1) \cong Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$: Schiffmann--Vasserot~2013. General quivers: Maulik--Okounkov~2019; Davison~2021 (critical CoHA). Stable envelopes and $R$-matrix: Maulik--Okounkov~2019 \S~4.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Mirror symmetry for quantum vertex chiral groups]
\label{conj:phys-3dm-mirror-qvcg}
Let $T$ and $T^!$ be 3d $\mathcal{N}=4$ mirrors (Intriligator--Seiberg~1996). The quantum vertex chiral groups of their branches are exchanged:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phys-mirror-qvcg}
G(M_H(T)) \;\cong\; G(M_C(T^!)), \qquad G(M_C(T)) \;\cong\; G(M_H(T^!)).
\end{equation}
Equivalently, there is an equivalence of $E_2$-chiral algebras
\[
\Phi(D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(M_H(T)))) \;\simeq\; \Phi(D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(M_C(T^!))))
\]
preserving braided monoidal structure, modular characteristic, and root datum. At the positive-half level, this is Yangian duality: $Y^+(\mathfrak{g}_Q) = \mathrm{CoHA}(M_H(T)) \cong \mathcal{A}^\hbar_{G^!,R^!} = Y^+(G(M_C(T^!)))$ where $(G^!, R^!)$ is the mirror gauge theory data.
\end{conjecture}

The non-trivial content of Conjecture~\ref{conj:phys-3dm-mirror-qvcg}: the Higgs side produces a CoHA (convolution on quiver representations) while the Coulomb side produces a BFN algebra (convolution on the affine Grassmannian); the conjecture asserts these are equivalent $E_2$-chiral algebras, with isomorphic root data. For type $A$ quivers, this is a theorem: the BFN algebra is a truncated shifted Yangian (Kamnitzer--Webster--Weekes--Yacobi~2021) and the isomorphism $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_n) \cong (\text{truncated shifted Yangian for } \mathfrak{gl}_N)$ is the mathematical incarnation of 3d mirror symmetry.

\begin{remark}[Hitchin moduli and geometric Langlands]
\label{rem:phys-3dm-hitchin}
For pure $G$-gauge theory on a curve $C$ (no matter), the Higgs branch is the Hitchin moduli space $M_H = T^*\mathrm{Bun}_G(C) = \mathrm{Higgs}(C,G)$, a $\mathrm{CY}_2$ space. The quantum vertex chiral group $G(M_H) = G(\mathrm{Higgs}(C,G))$ connects to geometric Langlands via Kapustin--Witten~2006: S-duality exchanges $G$ and $G^\vee$, which at the level of quantum vertex chiral groups should exchange $G(\mathrm{Higgs}(C,G))$ and $G(\mathrm{Higgs}(C,G^\vee))$. This is the conjectural quantum vertex chiral group incarnation of geometric Langlands (the Hitchin-mirror conjecture for $\mathrm{CY}_2$ targets).
\end{remark}
